# 15 — Decision log

Carried decisions come from `DESIGN.md` and `docs/design/*`; new ones are
marked. Each has a reason; reversing one means editing this table.

| # | Decision | Status | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | The three rules are the whole authoring interface; capability attaches to existing seams (workflow metadata, awaited objects, return values, exceptions) | carried | The usual way projects like this die is a fourth rule |
| D2 | Agents produce values; deterministic code routes. Agent-driven task movement is dropped, not deferred | carried | Every edge must be readable, testable, blameable |
| D3 | Native engine; "queue per node" is a query; the engine does not know agents exist | carried | Simplicity; agents are a capability of bodies |
| D4 | State flows through the append-only work log, payloads only for branch identity | carried | Concurrency-safe primitive; agents pull context themselves |
| D5 | Structured submissions validated at the endpoint (422 + schema), last valid wins, in-session repair turns | carried | Agent self-corrects in the same session; engine retry is the expensive path |
| D6 | Durability posture: re-execute the attempt after a crash; `human_input` re-attaches | carried | Two-phase suspension only if it hurts |
| D7 | Dispatch order: run position, explicit node priority, downstream-first, newest-first; retries keep `created` | carried | Under scarce inference an in-flight run drains before a new one starts |
| D8 | Named capacity pools, strict reservation, backend-agnostic, names reserved at registration | carried | Pools express a budget the operator knows; the scheduler never knows models |
| D9 | One request object for permissions, elicitations, node questions, HTTP asks; keyed answers; validation at the landing point | carried | One attention surface; no cross-talk under fan-out |
| D10 | Permission options chosen by kind, never by index; `ask` is the default policy; policies that cannot be honoured fail loudly | carried | claude-acp lists reject first; a silent denial reported `ok` |
| D11 | Config options resolved by category from what the session advertises; rejections logged | carried | Ids differ per agent; silent swallows hid a no-op |
| D12 | Plugins: declarations cross the wire, renderers do not; workflow is the host; actions allowed; escape hatch is a web component; explicit `ctx`; builtins dogfood; unknown kinds degrade | carried | Web-native frontend and a small core at once |
| D13 | The Textual TUI is deleted; the SPA is the operator UI; the CLI stays small | carried (plugins.md), executed | One frontend to maintain; the manifest is frontend-agnostic if a TUI ever returns |
| D14 | Vendor-specific adapters (pi, Claude, Docker) and stats parsing live in `examples/`, not the package | carried (memory) | Shipped library stays minimal |
| D15 | Waiting on a human releases the pool slot; resumed tasks re-admit ahead of new work | **new** | One unanswered question stalled the whole server under `workers=1` |
| D16 | Local first: loopback default with no operator auth; an operator token only when bound to a network; task tokens hashed, header-only, never returned in operator responses | **new** | Athanore runs on the operator's own machine; the cheap fixes (no token leak, header-only) cost nothing, the heavy ones stay opt-in |
| D17 | SQLAlchemy 2 async + Alembic; SQLite default, Postgres optional; cascade deletes | **new** | Hand-rolled migrations and blocking I/O on the loop do not scale past the MVP |
| D18 | Events are the audit trail and the realtime feed (outbox in the same transaction; SSE replays by cursor) | **new** | Store and feed can never disagree; late joiners catch up |
| D19 | Agent transcript is its own table with retention, not events | **new** | 2–3 events/s per task with no retention was unbounded growth |
| D20 | Per-node `retries` and `timeout` on the node-options seam | **new** | Requested repeatedly; same seam as `priority` |
| D21 | 422 for validation, stable error codes, ISO timestamps, ULID run ids | **new** | v1 is a new wire contract; parity with the MVP's ad-hoc 400s is not worth carrying |
| D22 | `queued` run status and `waiting` task status | **new** | Operators could not see "waiting for a slot" or "waiting for me" from the status alone |
| D23 | Run list order is `position`, not `priority` | **new** | Two things called priority (run and node) confused every reader |
| D24 | Frontend: Vite/React/TS, TanStack Router + Query, shadcn on Tailwind v4, cmdk, generated OpenAPI client (D49 names the generator) | **new** | Mainstream, documented, typed end to end; user preference for React + shadcn |
| D25 | Design tokens come from the Claude Design "Nocturne" system, imported to `docs/v1/design/` | **new** | Imported 2026-09-05; the mapping is in 10 §Design system |
| D26 | CLI on typer + rich with `--json` everywhere; `athanore serve` with entry-point discovery | **new** | Composable, discoverable; replaces `python -m workflow` |
| D27 | Truncation detection and token/cost stats go through a `SessionStatsProvider`; the pi session-file reader is an example | **new** | The core read pi's home directory; that is an adapter concern |
| D28 | Body size limit; rate limiting is a later seam | **new** | A runaway agent must not fill the disk; throttling is not worth its complexity on one machine |
| D29 | One process, one store, one bus in v1 | **new (explicit)** | Every internal boundary is an interface; distribution is a later seam |
| D30 | `template=` prompt files removed | **new** | Superseded by inlined prompts (spec `inline-prompts.md`); only tests used it |
| D31 | The SPA keeps the TUI's single-page shape from the design mock: run list left, one cycling pane right, overlays for everything else; no multi-page IA | **new** | The mock is the product owner's design; the TUI shape is what operators already know |
| D32 | The graph pane is the mock's vertical rail list, not a React Flow canvas; fan-out renders as indented sub-lists | superseded by D206 | Simpler, denser, and matches the mock; pipelines are mostly linear with loop-backs |
| D33 | The mock's `messages` pane (node → node) becomes the `requests` pane (human-in-the-loop history) | **new** | Athanore has no node-to-node messages; the work log is that channel; requests are the thing operators need to see |
| D34 | The mock's 1–10 priority slider on New Run becomes a top/bottom POSITION choice | **new** | Run ordering is a list position (D23), not a numeric priority |
| D35 | The mock's "hot-reloaded from workflows/" library is served from `GET /api/workflows/{name}/source`; hot reload is a later seam | **new** | Restart-to-reload is fine for v1; the source viewer is the valuable part |
| D36 | CRT chrome (scanlines, scan band, vignette, flicker) ships on by default per the mock, off under reduced motion, toggleable in settings | **superseded by D71** | It is the mock's signature; accessibility keeps the override |
| D37 | Design tokens are the Nocturne sheet with the mock's app overrides (JetBrains Mono, 12px, 2px radii, status colours); no light theme in v1 | **new** | The mock defines one dark theme; a light theme is a token-file change later |
| D38 | Task tokens are minted at claim, per attempt, hash-only at rest | **new (review 2026-09-05)** | Minting at enqueue with hash-only storage loses the clear text before the claim; per-attempt tokens also make recovered/retried attempts unforgeable from a dead one |
| D39 | Agent-facing routes live under `/api/agent/` on their own router | **new (review)** | One path with two response shapes chosen by credential is not expressible in OpenAPI and overloads the auth dependency |
| D40 | `task.stream` is an ephemeral bus/SSE event, never stored; the MVP's `transition` event folds into `task.enqueued` | **new (review)** | D19 moved the transcript out of `events` but still wrote one cursor event per flush; that kept most of the growth |
| D41 | Dispatch reads `runs.position` through the join; no `run_position` snapshot on tasks | **new (review)** | The claim query already joins `runs`; the snapshot and its reorder sync were legacy |
| D42 | Failure classes on rule 3: `GraphError` and `NonRetryable` dead-letter on the first attempt; everything else retries | **new (review)** | Retrying a routing defect burns inference; the exception type is metadata, not a fourth rule |
| D43 | Waiting tasks resume through a per-pool re-admit queue served before store claims; the node timeout clock pauses while waiting | **new (review)** | "Re-admit ahead of new work" needed a mechanism, since a `waiting` row is not `ready`; a day-long human wait must not fail a ten-minute agent cap |
| D44 | Node requests carry an ordinal per task row; a recovered body replays earlier answers | **new (review)** | The MVP re-attached only to the open request and re-asked every earlier one after a crash |
| D45 | SQLAlchemy Core, not the ORM; repositories return pydantic rows | **new (review)** | The design already forbade ORM instances crossing a boundary; the session/identity map then only adds traps |
| D46 | Agent stats are stored on `tasks.stats` as well as the log line and event | **new (review)** | Run totals are a `SUM`, not an event scan |
| D47 | `require_token` forces operator auth on a loopback bind | **new (review)** | A reverse proxy in front of loopback would otherwise expose the API unauthenticated |
| D48 | `Workflow` lives in `athanore/workflow.py`, composing the pure `graph` builder and the plugin declarations | **new (review)** | Plugin decorators on the graph object would have broken "graph imports nothing" |
| D49 | JSON-Schema-driven forms use `@rjsf/core` + `@rjsf/shadcn`; the client and TanStack Query options are generated by `@hey-api/openapi-ts`; fonts are bundled via fontsource | **new (review)** | A hand-written schema walker and hand-written query keys were wheels; a local tool must not phone Google Fonts |
| D50 | `panel` is a plain call; plugin manifest and panel kinds ship in the API phase so the SPA pane host is built on the manifest from day one | **new (review)** | Decorating an unused function was misleading; building panes twice (hard-coded, then on the manifest) was the plan's largest waste |
| D51 | Delete run is `D` (shift), keeping `d` for deny in the request panel | **new (review)** | Two destructive-adjacent meanings on one key, one focus ring apart |
| D52 | Shutdown interrupts attempts and leaves their rows `in_progress`/`waiting`; recovery resets them; `cancelled` is reserved for operator intent; `engine.stopping` records the interruption | **new (gap review 2026-09-05)** | 04 said shutdown records `cancelled` while recovery only reset `in_progress`, so a graceful stop lost work a crash would have recovered |
| D53 | Event payloads are typed per event name (18) and exposed in OpenAPI as a discriminated union on `name` | **new (gap review)** | The SPA, plugins, and CLI key on payload fields; "typed end to end" was false while `Event.data` was a dict |
| D54 | `settings.agent_command` (env/CLI only) swaps every `ACPAgent.command`; `FakeACPAgent` picks a scenario per `<workflow>.<node>`; examples ship scenarios | **new (gap review)** | "Examples green on the fake" was the phase gate with no mechanism behind it |
| D55 | Node-slot panel liveness rides on `GET /api/runs/{id}/graph` as `live`; the manifest stays static; `workflow`/`global` plugin contexts have no run and only run-independent services | **new (gap review)** | Liveness "computed server-side" had no transport |
| D56 | The agent-facing task read returns the work log without `kind=stats` lines, uncapped, oldest first | **new (gap review)** | Token counts are operator information; the MVP showed them and they distracted small models |
| D57 | `position {index}` is zero-based and clamped; `direction` at the ends is a 200 no-op; New Run "top" = index 0 | **new (gap review)** | The index base was unspecified and the New Run overlay depends on it |
| D58 | `run.output` is one value when the run ends with one terminal task and a list in branch order when several branches terminate independently; `RunDetail.outputs` always lists per-branch values; terminal tasks carry `terminal` | **new (gap review, revised)** | The MVP's "last branch wins" picked the value by a race; shape-by-structure is deterministic, and closing a fan-out with a join (D62) gives the single-value shape |
| D63 | Agents reach their task through three tooling tiers over one HTTP substrate: `mcp` (in-process MCP server passed on `new_session`, default when advertised), `native` (a harness extension, pi's in `examples/pi`), `http` (the curl block, fallback); the token leaves the prompt in the first two | **new (2026-09-05)** | Tools beat prompt-embedded curl for small models and for token hygiene, but pi has no MCP client, so MCP alone would strand the default agent |
| D62 | Fan-in: `@wf.node(join=True)` collects every branch of a fan-out; branch frames on tasks, durable `join_arrivals`, join dispatched in the arriving task's transaction; partial join at quiescence is `failed join_incomplete`; late arrivals recorded; `move` into a join rejected | **new (gap review)** | Without fan-in no node could run after all branches; metadata on the node seam plus one mechanism, not a fourth rule |
| D59 | The MVP findings the docs cite are folded into 20 so `docs/v1` is self-contained | **new (gap review)** | Agents building from the docs in a sandbox cannot read git-ignored files |
| D60 | Three timeouts nest (node, agent, wait); node timeout raises `TimeoutError` in the body, agent timeout raises `AgentError` after recording stats, both retryable; failed `AgentResult` is returned, not raised | **new (gap review)** | Which clock fired and what a body sees was implicit across 04 and 05 |
| D61 | Verbatim prompt blocks (19) are part of the contract and byte-tested; only the `/api/agent/` path and header-only token differ from the MVP | **new (gap review)** | Every example was tuned against that wording; drift would show up as example regressions with no test pointing at the cause |
| D64 | The dev stack (`compose.yaml`, `docker/dev/`, `scripts/`) lives in this repository, not in the separate `athanore-build` repo T000 assumed; one image backs the `dev` shell, the `app` server, the `web` server and both ACP agents, and the `scripts/` wrappers run the same on the host and inside the container | **new (2026-09-05)** | The gate an agent runs has to be the gate a human runs; a second repository holding the toolchain is a second thing to keep in step. T000's driver workflows (v0 dispatching the plan) remain out of scope here |
| D65 | This repository holds no MVP code: the MVP is the neighbouring checkout, read as the behavioural spec. The plan's coexistence items therefore do not apply — T002 drops `textual`, `netext` and `textual-dev` (nothing imports them and T055 would only remove them again) and `main.py` goes with the `uv init` placeholders; `athanore/__init__.py` is created in T002, ahead of T003, because the project has to be installable for the `examples` workspace member to resolve. "Port `tests/test_x.py`" means write the v1 test informed by that file and tick its row in `docs/porting-ledger.md` | **new (2026-09-05)** | 17 assumed a `v1` worktree of the MVP repository; a clean-slate repository makes half of T001-T002's instructions no-ops, and following them literally would add dead dependencies |
| D66 | Python 3.11+, not 3.13. The floor is set by `StrEnum` (events, error codes) and `asyncio.timeout` (D60's nested node/agent/wait timeouts); every runtime dependency already resolves on 3.11, so the floor costs no code. The dev stack and the CI default stay 3.13, and T006's CI runs a 3.11/3.12/3.13 matrix so the floor is tested | **new (2026-09-05)** | 3.13 excluded every current LTS distro's system Python for no benefit the code actually used; 3.10 would have cost the `async-timeout` backport and a `StrEnum` shim |
| D67 | v0 drives the v1 build from inside this repository (`driver/`, the `orchestrator` service, `./scripts/drive.sh`) rather than a separate `athanore-build` repo. v0 and v1 are both the `athanore` distribution, so they are kept apart by environment, never by renaming: v0 lives in the orchestrator's venv (a path source to the sibling v0 checkout, tagged `v0.0.12`) and is excluded from the v1 workspace; v1 lives in `athanore/dev`. v0 dispatches through `scripts/agent.sh` and gates through `scripts/test.sh`, so `compose.yaml` is the single definition of the sandbox for humans, for v0, and for v1 after the port. The driver listens on 4102 so it never collides with the v1 app on 4002 | **new (2026-09-05)** | Aliasing v0 would mean renaming its top-level package and every internal import, permanently diverging from the MVP, to solve a problem that environment isolation already solves |

| D68 | The build seat is branch-per-task, with three models and five deterministic nodes: `prepare` (refuse a dirty tree, branch `feat/<task-id>` from `main`), `implement` (DeepSeek V4 Pro), `gate` (git checks — on the branch, nothing uncommitted, a commit exists — then `./scripts/test.sh`, routing on the exit code), `review` and `qa` (Qwen3.8 Max: the branch diff, then the running feature), `approve` (`human_input` under `BUILDER_ATTENDED`), `merge` (`--no-ff` onto `main`, branch deleted). Loop-backs are counted per lane and in total; a run that fails, and an operator who answers `stop`, pause every queued run behind it. `WITH_BROWSERS` defaults to 1 so `qa` can drive the SPA | **new (2026-09-05)** | The first seat took the agent's word for its own commit and gated the working tree rather than the commit, so uncommitted work could pass; one model both wrote and reviewed; a failed task left the next one branching from a `main` without it; and `qwen/qwen3.8-max` is not an OpenRouter id, which v0 logs and downgrades to pi's default — every run so far was on the cheap model |
| D69 | v0's `AthanoreWorkflow.run()` shorthand is not ported to the v1 graph builder; the shorthand returns in T020 on the real `Workflow`, implemented against the `Server` that lands in T031 | **new (2026-09-05)** | v1 has no server until T031, and T020's `Workflow` is where the shorthand belongs; porting it onto the temporary `AthanoreWorkflow` alias now would import a server that does not exist yet |
| D70 | The plan carried two tasks numbered `T024a`: the failure-path runner task, and a superseded draft of the fan-in work that T024b and T024c were split out of. The duplicate is deleted; its one detail T024b did not state — `TaskRepo.enqueue` propagating `branch` for single transitions, retries, reruns and moves — is folded into T024b. The plan is 109 tasks, not 110 | **new (2026-09-05)** | A duplicate id makes `./scripts/drive.sh submit T024a` ambiguous and would have built the same fan-in twice, the second time against a tree that already had it |
| D71 | The mock drops the CRT chrome, every `text-shadow` glow, and the 2 px `--radius` override: `docs/v1/design/Athanore.dc.html` now uses Nocturne's 8 px radius throughout and keeps only the `ath-pulse` (running status) and `ath-caret` (agent stream) animations; the run list's selected row loses its gradient and inset glow for a flat `color-mix(accent 12%, surface)` tint with the accent left border. D36 is superseded; T058a loses `CrtChrome` and the `crt` preference, T057 loses the `glow-accent` utility and the radius override, T058's header loses its glow | **new (2026-09-05)** | The operator removed them from the design project; the mock is the reference for layout and copy (`AGENTS.md` §Conventions), so the normative sections of 10 follow it rather than the other way round |
| D72 | The `ARTIFICER_DB` legacy shim translates a bare filesystem path to `sqlite+aiosqlite:///{path}`; a value already carrying a `://` URL scheme is left untouched | **new (2026-09-05)** | v0 treated `ARTIFICER_DB` as a filesystem path (`athanore/server.py:139`), so assigning it verbatim to `db_url` produced a string no `create_async_engine` can open |
| D73 | `driver/` is linted with the rest of the tree (`ruff check .` and `ruff format --check .` include it; the first run's findings — an `asyncio.TimeoutError` alias, two over-long lines, one string-folding reformat — were fixed in place), but it is excluded from pyright and, via `root_package = "athanore"`, from import-linter. `*.md` is excluded from ruff entirely | **new (2026-09-05)** | `driver/` is Python in this repository and the boring choice is to hold it to the same ruff bar; fixing its three findings was cheaper than excluding it. pyright and import-linter cannot meaningfully check it: it imports the v0 `athanore` distribution (a path source to the sibling checkout, D67) whose symbols (`AthanoreServer`, `Pool`, …) do not exist in v1, so checking it against v1 would report only false positives. The markdown exclusion is the same reasoning applied to the formatter: `docs/v1` snippets are hand-aligned illustration, not importable source |

| D74 | The gate lives in `./scripts/test.sh`; no task adds a separate `scripts/gate.sh`. `test.sh` still execs one if it finds it, so a machine can carry a local override, but AGENTS.md §Commands and `test.sh`'s own comments no longer promise a handover that never happens | **new (2026-09-05)** | T005 was written expecting to add `gate.sh` and did not — nothing in its Do names it — leaving two documents pointing at a script that does not exist |
| D75 | The build seat runs on Claude: `implement` and `qa` on `opus[1m]`, `review` on Fable at effort `high`. Each role names an adapter (`BUILDER_*_KIND`: `pi` | `claude` | `claude-fable`) and a model valid for it, and the driver refuses a mismatched pair at startup. Fable needs its own compose service: `claude-agent-acp` builds its model menu from a fixed set (`opus[1m]`, `sonnet`, `haiku`, `default`) plus whatever `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` names in the container, and refuses `session/set_config_option` for anything else — measured, both `fable` and `claude-fable-5` are rejected on the default service. Claude authenticates from the `athanore-claude` volume, whose `settings.json` sets `permissions.defaultMode = bypassPermissions` | **new (2026-09-05)** | DeepSeek passed every gate but kept missing exhaustive spec compliance — a config field 02 requires, a secret accepted from a file 02 refuses, a layering contract weakened with a comment admitting it — which is the axis a stronger implementer should help with; and the container is the guardrail, so a permission round-trip per tool call buys nothing |
| D76 | T006's CI mirrors `./scripts/test.sh` (D74) rather than restating it: the `python` job's steps are the gate's steps in the gate's order, `web` and `contract` are written now but probe for `web/package.json` and `scripts/dump_openapi.py` and run nothing until T007/T008 create them, and `tests/test_ci_workflows.py` asserts the correspondence. `--cov` is added to the CI pytest run (`pytest-cov` and `pyyaml` join the dev group) but no `--cov-fail-under`: 13 §CI's thresholds (graph/engine/requests >= 95 %, overall >= 85 %) land with the code they measure. A pytest run that selects by marker and matches nothing exits 0, not 5 (`pytest_sessionfinish` in `tests/conftest.py`), scoped to `-m` so that a mistyped `-k` still fails | **new (2026-09-06)** | The gate and CI drifting apart is only ever discovered at a release, so the mirror is asserted by a test and the placeholder jobs are guarded rather than commented out or omitted. A coverage threshold on a package that is four modules old would fail on arithmetic, not on quality. `pytest -m postgres` selects nothing until T014 and would otherwise make every nightly run red before the suite it guards exists |
| D77 | The `web/` scaffold is `pnpm create vite --template react-ts` plus `pnpm dlx shadcn init --base radix --preset lyra`. The shadcn CLI no longer has a `--style` flag — `new-york` is the only style — and its `lyra` preset is Radix primitives, Phosphor icons and JetBrains Mono, which is 10 §Stack exactly. The linter is the scaffold's own oxlint, run as `oxlint --deny-warnings`; TypeScript 6 refuses `baseUrl`, so the `@/*` alias is `paths` alone; tests are a third project (`tsconfig.test.json`) so that `node` types reach the suites and not the app; and `<html>` carries `dark` permanently, since Nocturne is a dark theme with no light one and shadcn components branch on the class | **new (2026-09-06)** | The plan's `--style new-york` and its separate `@phosphor-icons/react` / `@fontsource-variable/jetbrains-mono` installs describe a CLI two majors old; `lyra` is the same choices, made by the tool. 02 §Library choices fixes no JavaScript linter, so the boring choice is the one the scaffold ships, made to fail the gate on a warning |
| D78 | `web/src/styles/theme.css` is generated by `web/scripts/gen-theme.mjs` and its freshness is asserted by `web/src/styles/theme.test.ts`, which runs the generator with `--check` inside `pnpm test`, rather than by a new CI job. The generated file copies Nocturne's `--color-*`, `--space-*`, `--shadow-*` and `--ath-*` declarations verbatim and then declares the shadcn variables on top of them; `@theme inline` exposes **only** the shadcn roles 10 §Tokens → shadcn defines, not Nocturne's own ramps, because Tailwind v4 has one `--color-*` namespace and `--color-accent` cannot be both Nocturne's #9184d9 and shadcn's 16 % tint. `--radius: 8px` is written literally, since `--radius-*` is not one of the four copied families and shadcn re-derives `--radius-md` from `--radius`; `--ath-radius: 2px` is copied with the rest and left unused (D71). The `-foreground` companions the table does not name are derived from the same tokens, and `sidebar-*`, `--chart-4` and `--chart-5` are omitted rather than left pointing at variables nothing declares | **new (2026-09-06)** | The gate is the definition of green (D74) and CI mirrors it (D76), so a staleness check that lives in `pnpm test` is caught by both without a fourth CI job to keep in step. Nocturne's `--color-neutral-*` and `--shadow-*` do land on Tailwind's own namespaces, and because `theme.css` is imported unlayered it wins: `border-neutral-800` and `shadow-md` render Nocturne's values, which is the intent |
| D79 | `artifacts = ["athanore/web/dist/**"]` moves from `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]` to `[tool.hatch.build]`, and `vite.config.ts` carries a build plugin that rewrites `athanore/web/dist/.gitkeep` after `emptyOutDir` has removed it. pnpm's workspace root is the checkout, so `/node_modules/` and `/.pnpm-store/` join `.gitignore` | **new (2026-09-06)** | `uv build` builds the wheel from the sdist, and the sdist honoured only the wheel target's `artifacts`, so the published wheel had `athanore/web/dist/assets/` but no `index.html` — T007's exit condition, failing silently. Vite's `emptyDir` skips `.git` and nothing else, so every build deleted the tracked `.gitkeep` and left the checkout dirty, which the build seat's `gate` node reads as uncommitted work. pnpm parks its store in the checkout because the container's store volume is on a different filesystem from the bind-mounted worktree and hardlinks cannot cross it |
| D80 | The OpenAPI document is titled `Athanore` and versioned with the installed distribution version (08 §Versioning); `create_app` reads it from `importlib.metadata`, which is also what `/api/health` reports. Operation ids are FastAPI's defaults, so the generated client exports `healthApiHealthGet`. `pnpm -C web gen` is `uv run --project .. ../scripts/dump_openapi.py && openapi-ts`, so the snapshot and the client cannot be regenerated apart, and `@hey-api/openapi-ts` runs with `postProcess: []`. The generated tree `web/src/api/gen` is its own TypeScript project, `web/tsconfig.gen.json`, referenced by the app and test projects | **new (2026-09-06)** | 08 fixes neither a document title nor an operation-id scheme; a name is the boring choice, and a naming policy for generated symbols belongs to T048 (OpenAPI metadata) with the tags and security schemes, not to the pipeline that carries them. openapi-ts bundles its fetch runtime into the output as of v0.73, and that runtime is not `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`-clean; T007 chose that flag deliberately, so the generated tree gets its own contract rather than the SPA losing a check. A formatter over generated output is one more tool that can differ between a laptop and CI, and the freshness check compares bytes |
| D81 | The event payloads of 18 live in `athanore/events/payloads.py` (T009's Do), and 18 §Typing's `athanore/api/schemas/events.py` re-exports them when T044 needs them. Fields 18 types with a domain enum (`RunStatus`, `TaskStatus`, `RequestMode`, `RequestKind`, `RequestSource`, `LogAuthor`, `LogKind`) are typed `str` there; narrowing them is the API layer's to do. `AgentStats` carries `denied_permissions`, which 05 §Stats entry lists and 18's summary of it omits. `EventEnvelope` is a union of one envelope class per name (`RunCreatedEvent`, …, `PluginEvent`) discriminated by a callable on `name`, not a single model with a discriminated `data`: pydantic cannot discriminate a field on a sibling, and 18's payload objects do not carry the name | **new (2026-09-06)** | `athanore.events` and `athanore.store` are independent siblings of the bottom tier, so `events` importing the enums would break the layering contract, and the enums do not exist until T010 in any case. A field 05 requires and 18 forgot is an addition, which is a minor change (18 §Rules 1), where dropping it would lose a measurement the stats line already prints. The callable discriminator is also what gives `plugin.*` its fall-through to `PluginEvent` |

(5) The loop takes a pool's free slots *before* it claims and releases the surplus after (D107), so `free()` read at the wrong instant sees a reservation about to be given back; the assertion is about the slot coming back, which is what the poll says. Its stats reason is `transport`: of 05's seven it is the one that means *the conversation broke*. It is the same failure mode reported by the one source that can see it directly; the provider stays the path for the case ACP cannot see (a turn consumed by reasoning, reported `end_turn`; D27). (7) **ACP `usage` is summed over the turns of a run**, repair turns included — `merge_usage` is documented as "what this run's turns reported", and a run that took two turns spent both. The transcript belongs to the attempt and the runner closes it in the `finally` of every attempt; a façade that closed it would leave the second of two agents in one body with nowhere to write. 05 §Session lifecycle step 7 says "flush"; the plan's `stream.close()` was shorthand. (10) **`StreamService.append` holds a lock across the counter and the buffer write.** The producer is an ACP callback and the SDK dispatches every `session/update` as its own task, so a turn's chunks arrive concurrently: without it the first several appends all found `_seq` unresolved, all read the same `last_seq` and all claimed the same number — a `UNIQUE` violation on `(task_id, seq)` that failed the whole batch — and the buffer ended up in whatever order the reads finished in. | D129 | The auth spine, and the ten choices T043 made. (1) **`create_app` is where a server refuses to start**, not just `Server` (T051): `check_operator_token` raises `MissingOperatorToken` from the factory, so the CLI, the programmatic host and a test all get the same refusal from the same place. It fires whenever `auth_mode` is `"token"` and no token is configured — so `require_token` on a loopback bind is refused too, not only a non-loopback one. (2) **`VERSION` moves from `athanore.api.app` to `athanore.api`.** The routers report it and `app` imports the routers, so a constant every router reads has to sit where nothing imports back; `athanore.api.app` re-exports it, so nothing that imported it before changed. (3) **`Health`'s three counts are optional and omitted** when the application was built without the collaborator that answers them. 08 §System lists them unconditionally, but a bare `create_app()` — the OpenAPI dump, a unit test — has no store and no engine, and 02 §Real data only forbids zero-filling: "no store to ask" and "nothing running" are different facts. A served application always carries both, so the wire a client meets is 08's. (4) **`tasks_in_progress` counts `in_progress` only**, not `waiting`. The name is literal, and 04 §Shutdown's drain — pause every run, wait for the count to reach zero — would never finish while one question sat unanswered, since a waiting task holds no slot and needs an operator, not time. (5) **A missing `X-Athanore-Token` is 422, not 403.** The header is declared required, as 17 §T043 spells the signature, so FastAPI reports it with the one validation shape of 08 §Conventions and names the header that is absent; 403 stays the answer for a token that was presented and refused, and the three ways it can be refused — unknown, another task's, an attempt that has ended — share one message, because which one it was is not information the door should hand out. (6) **`/api/me` reports `authenticated: true` when `auth` is `off`.** The field says whether the caller has operator rights, and on a plain loopback bind every caller does (12 §Posture); the SPA branches on the pair, so `{"auth": "off", "authenticated": false}` would be a token screen nobody could satisfy. (7) **Redaction is a `logging.Filter` on the shared handler**, not a structlog processor. It runs before formatting, which is the only point where a foreign record's `args` and a structlog record's event dict are both still Python objects. A record that carries `args` is redacted through its *formatted* message and kept with no args left: rewriting the format string alone would delete the `%s` its argument was going to fill and turn `log.warning("Authorization: Bearer %s", token)` into a `TypeError`. (8) **It redacts more than the two names the task lists.** `Authorization` and the `access_token` query are the two 17 §T043 names; `X-Athanore-Token` and a mapping key that *names* a credential are added, because 12 §Hygiene says never log tokens and a task token is one — and a credential passed as a structlog keyword is a bare value with nothing around it for a pattern to match. (9) **The counts are two repository methods, `RunRepo.count_running` and `TaskRepo.count_in_progress`**, rather than a `select` in the router: 07 §Repositories owns the queries, and a router that wrote SQL would be the first one to. (10) **`Health`, `Me` and `PoolHealth` live in `routers/system.py`.** `athanore/api/schemas/` is T044's to fill, and these three are the system router's own wire types. `PoolHealth` restates `PoolSnapshot`'s two fields as a pydantic model rather than using the `TypedDict`, which pydantic only accepts from `typing_extensions` below Python 3.12 and the floor is 3.11 (D66). (3) is 02 §Real data only met for the first time in the API — the alternative, `runs_running: 0` on an application with no database, is the exact shape of a monitor reporting a dead server as drained. (4) is the number an operator's shutdown loop waits on, so it has to mean the thing the loop can wait out. (7) is a defect the obvious implementation has: the redaction that reads best — rewrite the message, redact the arguments — breaks every `%`-formatted log line that hides its credential in an argument, which is precisely uvicorn's access line. | D131 | The runs router, and the nine choices T044a made. (1) **`/graph` groups `branches` by the whole branch-frame stack**, and `from_task` is the innermost frame's `fanout`. 08 §Graph semantics says `from_task` is "the `lineage.from` of the first task in the branch chain whose parent returned a list": the first task of *this* branch's chain is the one the innermost fan-out enqueued, and its `lineage.from` is that fan-out. Two branches of one fan-out are two entries with the same `from_task`; a node's retries inside one branch are two ids in one entry. (2) **`arrivals` picks the innermost open fan-out by nesting depth** — the length of the fan-out task's own branch stack — and by the highest task id on a tie. (3) **`traversed` is counted off the event log, paged**, 1000 rows at a time, accumulating counts rather than rows. (4) **`state` precedence is `NodeState`'s member order**, not a table in the router: every member but `idle` is the name of a task status. (5) **An edge is `back` before it is `join`**, in 08's own order. (6) **Nodes come out in generation order and, within a generation, in declaration order.** (7) **A run whose workflow is not registered here is a 404 `unknown_workflow` from `/graph`**, and only from `/graph`; the list shows it with `unregistered: true`. (2) 08 says "innermost" and depth is what innermost means; the id is a tiebreak, not the rule. (3) A run's history is unbounded and a single capped read would undercount an edge silently, which is worse than a second round trip. (4) One declaration cannot drift from itself. (5) An arrow to a generation at or below its source is a loop however its target is declared, and the crossing count is right either way: a transition into a join enqueues nothing, so the `join.arrived` arm is what counts it. (6) It is the order 10 §Graph pane draws, and declaration order within a rank keeps the author's ordering visible. (7) There is no graph to project onto, and inventing a shape for a workflow this process does not have would be the opposite of 01 §Real data only. (8) The task list is already in hand, so the fields cost one query rather than a store method T044a's plan does not fence. (9) "Nothing was in flight" and "three agents were mid-turn" are the same call and very different events, and it is the one part of the outcome the caller could not predict. (2) **`?pending` on `GET /api/requests` defaults to `true`.** 08 §Requests names the route "the inbox"; `?pending=false` widens it to every request, and `?run=` scopes either. (3) **`?limit=` on `/stream` defaults to 500 and caps at 5000**, the pair `/api/runs/{id}/events` already uses (D131 (9)). (4) **The two request reads come from the store and `/answer` goes through the service.** Reading is `RequestRepo.list_views`' join — the same read `GET /api/runs/{id}/requests` makes — and answering is `RequestService.answer` reached through the engine's `RequestBackend` port, which is the only member of the request service the engine holds. (5) **`/answer` answers with the view re-read after the service call**, not with a view composed from the `AnswerRow`. (6) **A missing collaborator is a 404, never a 500**: a store-less application has no task and no request, an engine composed without a request service has nothing to answer, and the inbox of a store-less application is `[]` — the shape `routers/runs.py` already uses. (7) **`live`'s field description in `athanore/api/schemas/tasks.py` is corrected** to name both statuses; that file is not in the plan's list and is the only one outside it that this task touches. | **new (2026-09-07)** | (1) A waiting attempt is parked on a request whose answer is what makes the agent go on writing, and the docked request panel sits under that very transcript (10 §Panes) — a `live` that excluded `waiting` would stop the SPA polling at the one moment the stream is about to grow again, and 17's phrase is the informal "still going", not the enum member. (2) Both consumers 06 §Surfaces names — the SPA's global requests pane and `athanore requests` — want exactly the requests a person can still act on, a run's whole history already has a route of its own, and an unpaged read across every run should be the thing a caller asks for rather than the thing it gets by accident. (3) One page of history is never larger than one SSE replay, and a second constant pair would be a second thing to keep in step. (4) The read is a join three tables wide that the store already expresses; the write is the mode's validation, the registered validator, the `request.answered` event that wakes the waiter and the transaction around all of it, which is 06 §Service's and not a router's to restate. (5) `pending`, `answer` and `answered_by` are all part of what changed and two of them are computed by the join, so re-reading is the only way the response is the request as it now is. (6) An application without a collaborator is a real thing — the OpenAPI dump is one — and "no such task" is the honest answer where "the server is misconfigured" is not the caller's problem. (7) A wire description that contradicts the value behind it is published into the generated client, which is worse than the one-line edit. |
| D133 | The agent router, and the eight choices T045 made. (1) **The agent's response models live in `athanore/api/schemas/agent.py`** — `AgentTask`, `Ask`, `AskOption`, `AskOut`, `AnswerPoll` — a module the plan's file list does not name, because 08 names no model for these bodies and the package is one module per resource (D130). The request body sits there rather than in `bodies.py`, which is the operator's POSTs and says so. (2) **`Depends(task_auth)` is a parameter on every route, not a router dependency**, so each handler gets the `TaskRow` the door already read instead of reading it again; FastAPI caches the dependency, so it still runs once. (3) **"409 if the task is not in progress" is "no live context"**: `engine.live.context_for(id)` is the only thing that knows whether an attempt of this task is running *here*, and after a crash the row still says `in_progress` until recovery turns it back into `ready`. The precedence is the door first — a token from a finished attempt is 403 (12 §Task tokens), never 409. (4) **`/log` and `/ask` need the live context too**, for the same reason `/submit` does: both write through the attempt's own services (`LogService`, `TaskRequests`), which is what gives the entry this task's node and the request `source="agent"`. `GET /api/agent/tasks/{id}` needs none and works from the store alone, reporting `output_schema` only when a context is live and has declared a model. (5) **An ill-formed ask is 422 `validation`, where v0 answered 400.** Every other body in the API is a pydantic model and 08 §Conventions maps a body that misfits to 422; a second convention for one route would be one the SPA and the CLI have to know about. `400 invalid_option` stays what it is — an *answer* naming an option the request did not offer. (6) **An option object requires `option_id`.** 08 writes `[str | {option_id, name, kind?}]`; `name` defaults to the id, and a bare string is an option that is its own id and label. v0's fallback of reading `name` as the id is a leniency 08 does not state. (7) **The long-poll answers `{request_id, answered, answer, answered_by}`**, with `request_id` beside 08's three fields (v0 sent it too) and `answer` folded the way `RequestView` folds it — the `option_id` of an `options` request, the value of a `text` or `form` one. `?wait=` is **clamped** to `[0, 120]` rather than refused, and a poll that finds an answer unregisters the request's validator, because this poll is the waiter. (8) **`MockAgent(submit=…)` posts to the endpoint** (`{ctx.api_base}/api/agent/tasks/{id}/submit`, `X-Athanore-Token`), which is what T037 deferred and D122 (6) marked with `# T045`. A 422 is an outcome and is left to `attach()` to report; any other status is raised as an `AgentError` naming it. `tests/testing/conftest.py` therefore grows a `served_context` — a claimed attempt, registered on `engine.live`, with a real `create_app()` listening on loopback — beside the plain `context` the other doubles use, which is the split `tests/agents/conftest.py` already draws. | **new (2026-09-07)** | (1) The models are the wire's and the wire is 08's; putting them beside the operator's in `bodies.py` would put the one body an agent sends in the file whose first line says it is the operator's. (2) The row is the task the token authenticated, and re-reading it would be a second read that could disagree with the first. (3) A submission accepted into a process that is not running the attempt is a value nothing will route on: the body that would have read it died with the process, and recovery re-runs the attempt from the top. Checking the status column instead would accept exactly that submission. (4) Both writes are transactions the engine owns and both carry events the SPA re-renders on; an API that wrote the rows itself would be a second implementation of `log.appended` and of `source="agent"`. (5) and (6) are 08 read literally where v0 was lenient, and the leniency is the kind that hides a caller's mistake — an option list whose ids are its labels answers differently from one whose ids are absent. (7) The agent asked which request it opened and 19's loop repeats the call; clamping keeps `?wait=3600` a two-minute call rather than a 422 the model has to parse, and folding the answer once means the operator's view of a request and the agent's view of its answer cannot drift. (8) is the whole point of the deferral: the double's claim is that what it leaves out is the subprocess, and a submission that never crossed the wire left the endpoint's validation, its 422 and its transaction untested by everything that used the double. |
| D134 | The MCP server, and the nine choices T045a made, plus two files its list did not name. (1) **The tools are the routes.** Each tool calls the very function `athanore/api/routers/agent.py` registers as a route, with the body that route would have parsed, so nothing is reachable through MCP that is not reachable through `/api/agent/` and the two cannot drift. (2) **Auth is a pure-ASGI guard in front of the mounted app** (`mcp.TaskTokenGuard`), not a check inside each tool: the token is resolved once, before a byte of JSON-RPC is parsed, and the attempt is left on the request for the tools to read. `deps.live_task` is factored out of `task_auth` as the shared predicate — hence the two unlisted files, `athanore/api/deps.py` and `athanore/api/routers/agent.py` (whose `_maybe_live` becomes `maybe_live`). (3) **A missing header is the same 403 as a wrong one**, where the REST route answers 422: MCP has no signature for FastAPI to report a missing header against, and which of the two it was is not information the door hands out (12). (4) **The transport is stateless streamable HTTP**, DNS-rebinding protection off, `max_request_body_size = body_limit`: a task token is authenticated per request and has no session to outlive, the posture is 12's, and the body cap is the application's one number. (5) **The endpoint is a plain Starlette `Route`, not a `Mount`** — an exact path, where a mount would also answer `/mcp/agent/anything` and redirect the bare path. (6) **The tool listing is built per request**: `submit_result`'s input schema is the live context's `output_model` schema (a free object when none), and `ask_operator`/`wait_answer` are listed only with `ask_policy == "http"` — calling either anyway still gets `/ask`'s 403. (7) **A refusal is an `isError` result carrying 08 §Conventions' error body**, and bad arguments are the API's 422 shape rather than the protocol's `invalid params`; the one exception is a rejected submission, which is 08 §MCP's `{ok: false, errors, schema}`. Every result is said twice — `structuredContent` and the same object as JSON text — because not every harness reads the first. (8) **`wait` is clamped, not refused**, as on `/requests/{rid}`; `ask_operator` may wait inside the one call, because a tool call is already a turn the model waits in. (9) **OpenAPI carries a hand-written path item** injected by wrapping `app.openapi`: a mounted ASGI app is invisible to the generator, and the 403 body is written out inline rather than adding a component the rest of the document does not have yet. `create_app`'s lifespan now runs the session manager, which is the one collaborator the application owns | **new (2026-09-07)** | (1) is the whole of "one wire contract": a second implementation of the same five operations would be a second contract to keep in step, and 08 §MCP says the server is "a thin adapter over the same services the REST routes call". (2) and (3) keep the credential's meaning in one function, so a token cannot mean one thing to REST and another to MCP. (4) matches the lifetime a task token already has. (6) is 05 §Tooling tiers verbatim — the model sees the submission schema as a tool definition and fixes a misfit in the same turn — and absence is the enforcement the prompt already assumes. (7) is what a model can act on: it reads tool results, not status lines. (9) is the only way the snapshot can record a route the generator cannot see |
| D135 | The SSE endpoint, and the thirteen choices T046 made. (1) **`resync` is a frame with a body**: `event: resync`, no `id:`, `data: {"reason": "replay_capped"|"overflowed"}`. (2) **A replay past the cap sends no history at all** — `cap + 1` rows are read, and if the page overran, the endpoint yields `resync` and nothing else, leaving `last_id` at the cursor the client arrived with. (3) **Overflow is reported once per subscription**, checked at the top of each turn of the live loop rather than polled. (4) **`after` beats `Last-Event-ID`**, and a `Last-Event-ID` that is not a non-negative integer is a 422 `validation` naming the header. (5) **A `names` glob containing `[` or `]` is a 422 at the door**, in the route function, before the response has begun. (6) **`names=` with nothing in it is no filter**, matching `EventRepo.list_after`'s reading of an empty `patterns`. (7) **The `run` filter is applied to the live half in the endpoint**, by comparing `event.run_id`; the bus filters by name only. (8) **The bus is the engine's, or the store's if there is no engine**; an application with neither opens the stream, replays nothing and holds it open on the keep-alive. (9) **The replay's read runs shielded, in its own task.** (10) **Frames are serialised through the same `EventEnvelope` `TypeAdapter` as `GET /api/runs/{id}/events`.** (11) **The 200 declares `text/event-stream` with an empty media-type object** — the media type, and no schema. (12) **`tests/api/test_auth.py` loses the stub `/api/events` T043 stood in for**, and the accepting half of that route's matrix moves to `tests/api/test_sse.py`. (13) **The route is excluded from the TypeScript client** — `web/openapi-ts.config.ts` gains `parser.filters.operations.exclude: ['GET /api/events']`, and `filters.orphans: true` beside it. | **new (2026-09-07)** | (1) A frame whose data buffer is empty is *not dispatched* by a browser's `EventSource`, so a bare `event: resync` would never reach the handler 10 §Realtime installs for it; the reason then also makes a stream that resyncs often diagnosable. (2) 08 says the client "refetches": a truncated history it would believe was complete is worse than none, and one query answers both "the page" and "is there more". (3) `Subscription.overflowed` is sticky by D85 and nobody clears it, so a per-event check would be a storm rather than a signal; and the flag is only ever set while the queue is full, so a subscription that overflowed always has something to deliver and always reaches the check before it blocks again. (4) The header is the browser's own reconnect cursor and only ever echoes an id this endpoint sent; a client that made one up is better told than silently replayed from the beginning, and an explicit `after` is what the caller meant. (5) D87 refuses a character class in the store's glob, so a replay could not be made to agree with the live filter; raising it in the route means the client gets a JSON body rather than a stream that opens and dies. (6) "Give me no names" is never what a caller means, and the two halves must read the parameter the same way. (7) A run is not part of an event's name, so there is no pattern that expresses it. (8) A served application always has both collaborators; a bare `create_app()` is the OpenAPI dump, and a stream that says nothing has happened is the truth there rather than a 500 about the server's own wiring. (9) The replay is the one place the stream holds a pooled connection, and a client abandoning the request during it is ordinary — a browser closing a tab does it. Cancellation lands inside the read and then inside the connection's own `close`, and SQLAlchemy terminates a connection that was never checked back in, complaining from the garbage collector long afterwards; the shield lets the query and its cleanup finish while the caller is cancelled at once. `test_a_disconnect_during_the_replay_returns_the_connection` fails without it. (10) One wire contract: a client that caught up over REST and then subscribed must not meet a second shape, and a hand-built frame would render `created` differently from the endpoint next door. (11) The generator turns it into an SSE-aware client method; a JSON schema on the response would be a fiction, because the body is frames rather than a document. T048 owns the rest of the OpenAPI metadata. (12) T043's stub shadowed nothing once the real route landed — FastAPI matches the first registration — and `httpx.ASGITransport` runs an application to completion before it returns a response, so a `GET` that opens a real stream never comes back. The refusals stay where the door is the subject; the openings moved to where a harness can read a live stream. (13) The SPA reads this route with `EventSource` and never with the fetch client (10 §Realtime, T060), and `@hey-api/openapi-ts` reads the `after` parameter as a pagination cursor and emits infinite-query options whose `const { data } = await …` does not typecheck against a `ServerSentEventsResult` — an infinite query over a stream being meaningless in the first place. The document still carries the operation, which is where the contract lives; only a client method nothing could call is dropped. `orphans: true` is there because naming any filter turns the parser's orphan pruning on, which would otherwise silently delete the event payload types the generator leaves opaque. |
| D136 | The static surface, and the eight choices T047 made. (1) **The SPA is `app.router.default`, not a `Mount` or a catch-all route at `/`.** Starlette consults the router's fallback only after every route has failed to match, so routing that comes first is untouched — a method an API route lacks is still its 405, a trailing slash is still its 307 — and, decisively, a route registered *after* `create_app` still answers: the plugin routes of T070, the asset mounts of T071, and the guarded route `tests/api/test_auth.py` hangs off a real application. (2) **`/api/…` and `/plugins/…` are reserved**: unmatched, they answer 404 in the shape of 08 §Conventions (`code: not_found`, the path in the message) rather than with `index.html`. (3) **Only `GET` and `HEAD` reach the SPA**; an unrouted `POST` is the same JSON 404. (4) **The CSP of 12 §Plugins goes on in a `StaticFiles` subclass** (`PolicyFiles.get_response`), not in a middleware, so it rides the SPA's document, the SPA's assets and a plugin's assets and nothing else. (5) **A missing `dist/index.html` is a 200 HTML page naming `pnpm -C web install` and `pnpm -C web build`**, and the directory is read per request, so a build under a running server needs no restart. (6) **`CORSMiddleware` is added after the body cap** — therefore outside it — with `allow_credentials=False` and the configured origins, never `*`, and nothing at all when `cors_origins` is empty. (7) **`mount_plugin_assets(app, workflow, directory)` is the seam T071 fills**, with `check_dir` on so a declared assets directory that does not exist raises at registration (09 §Registration and validation). (8) **The build inlines nothing**: `web/vite.config.ts` sets `build.assetsInlineLimit: 0`, so every font subset is a file under `/assets` rather than a `data:` URL in the CSS. | **new (2026-09-07)** | (1) is the whole of the choice: a `Mount` at `/` matches every path, and the failure it produces is silent — a plugin route registered a moment later returns the SPA's document with a 200, which reads as a frontend bug for as long as it takes someone to notice the response was HTML. The fallback expresses what was meant, "an unmatched path is the client router's", instead of "every path is the SPA's, and mind the order". (2) and (3) are the same failure at the other end: an endpoint typo answering `200 text/html`, or a missing asset arriving at a `<script type="module">` as HTML, is a client bug that looks like a parsing bug, and both are the maddening kind because nothing logs an error. Neither costs the trailing-slash redirect, which Starlette runs before it consults the fallback at all. (4) A policy is a claim about a document, and the API's JSON is not one; putting it in a middleware would also put it on responses this module does not own. (5) A fresh checkout that has not run a build is the ordinary state of a Python-side task, not an error, and a stack trace or a 404 there tells the operator nothing about the two commands that fix it; per-request reading means `pnpm -C web build` in another terminal is enough. (6) The one caller CORS exists for is the Vite dev server (12 §Operator token), and a wildcard default would undo the loopback-first posture for every deployment that never asked for it; credentials stay off because no cookie is used, so there is no CSRF surface to open. Outside the cap, a 413 still carries the headers the browser needs to read it. (7) 09 says a missing assets directory fails registration, and that is a startup error only if the mount refuses to be built. (8) This is the first task that serves the SPA under the policy, and it is where the two met: Vite's default 4096-byte limit inlined the smallest JetBrains Mono subset (cyrillic-ext, ~2.7 kB of base64) into the built CSS, and `font-src 'self'` does not permit `data:`, so a browser blocked that face on every page load and fell back to a system font for Cyrillic-extended glyphs. The two documents already claim the fixed behaviour — 12 §Plugins' "fonts are bundled, so nothing loads from a third party" and 10's "`font-src 'self'` holds" — so the build was made to match them rather than the policy relaxed with `data:` for a self-inflicted reason. The cost is one request for the smallest of six subsets |
| D137 | The OpenAPI metadata, and the five choices T048 made. (1) **All eight tags of 08 §OpenAPI are declared up front**, `plugins` included, before T049a gives it an operation. (2) **Security is per route, through `openapi_extra`**: `athanore/api/openapi.py` holds the two schemes and `secure(router, requirement)` writes the requirement onto every route of a router, while `install_openapi(app)` puts `components.securitySchemes` on the document by wrapping `app.openapi` — the seam `api/mcp.py` already uses. `operatorBearer` is declared unconditionally, although the server only enforces it on a network bind. (3) **The 422 of 08 §Conventions is declared on the routers, not left to FastAPI**, and `install_openapi` takes it back off the operations that have neither a parameter nor a body. (4) **`ApiError` is a wire model** (`athanore/api/schemas/errors.py`), named after the schema 08 §OpenAPI names and therefore sharing a name with the *exception* in `athanore/api/errors.py`; it is the one model this package does not re-export from `athanore.api.schemas`, and its `errors` field reuses 18's `ValidationError` rather than restating the `loc`/`msg`/`type` triple. (5) **`PluginEvent.name` is `EventName | str`** rather than `str` — the one variant of the union whose name is not a `Literal` — and `EventModel` overrides `__get_pydantic_json_schema__` to drop the serializer's return type from the serialization schema and to restate `required` as the fields the wire always carries. | **new (2026-09-07)** | (1) The tag vocabulary is part of the contract; a list that only shows the tags some route happens to carry is not one anybody can read off the document, and the alternative is editing this list from the middle of another task. (2) A security requirement in OpenAPI says what a credential is and where it goes, not that a given deployment demands one — a document whose shape depended on the bind host would be a contract that depended on how it was served, and the snapshot is taken from a bare `create_app()`. (3) FastAPI hard-codes `HTTPValidationError` — `{"detail": [...]}` — for any operation with something to parse, and that is not what `validation_error_handler` sends. Worse, the definitions it injects are merged over the generated ones **by name**, and one of them is `ValidationError`, which is also 18's triple: left alone, FastAPI's version silently replaced 18's, and `submission.rejected` was documented with an `input` and a `ctx` it never carries. Declaring the response stops the injection at the source; pruning afterwards is FastAPI's own rule (`parameters or requestBody`) applied honestly, so `GET /api/workflows` does not advertise a refusal it cannot answer. (4) A component takes the name of the class it was generated from, so the schema 08 names `ApiError` has to be a class called `ApiError`; the two live in different modules and a router that writes the bare name means the exception, which is why the model is imported by path. One function projects pydantic's errors onto `loc`/`msg`/`type` and its result is both the 422 body and the `submission.rejected` payload, so two schemas would be two names for one shape. (5) 08 §OpenAPI wants the event-name enum *referenced* from an event's `name`, and a `Literal[EventName.run_created]` renders as a bare `const`. The open end of the union is the only field that can carry the reference, and it costs nothing to: `anyOf: [EventName, string]` admits exactly the strings `string` admits, so the schema is no wider than it was and the document gains the vocabulary the TypeScript union of 13 §Contract tests is generated from. Declaring it on `EventFrame` and narrowing per variant would read better and does not typecheck — a pydantic field is a mutable attribute, so pyright makes every `Literal` override an error. It is reachable at all only because of the schema override: `_omit_absent` is annotated `-> dict[str, Any]`, pydantic takes a model serializer's return type as the model's serialization schema, and OpenAPI renders responses in serialization mode — so every event was `{"type": "object", "additionalProperties": true}`, which is neither the `oneOf` per event 18 §Typing promises nor anything the SPA could discriminate. This supersedes the aside in D135 (13) that called those payload types opaque; `orphans: true` stays for the reason D135 gave it. The same override has to restate `required`, because validation mode drops every field that has a default and a default is only half of what makes a field absent on the wire: `_omit_absent` omits a key only when its value is `None` **and** the field admits `None`, so the document requires everything but `id`, `run_id` and `task_id` — the three 18 §Envelope allows to be missing — and the `name` each envelope pins itself with stays required, which is what makes the union tagged rather than merely tagged-when-present |
| D138 | The plugin declarations, and the nine choices T049 made. (1) **The sixth validation check is the `source` one**: a panel's `source` must name one of this workflow's routes, or — for a `form` panel — one of its actions. 09 §Registration states five refusals in one sentence and this one in its table ("`source` (a route)", "an action name"); 17 §T049 asks for six, and the document now lists all six in the order `validate` runs them. (2) **Registration refuses with `PluginValidationError(ValueError)`, not `PluginError`.** `PluginError(status, message)` carries an HTTP status because a *handler* raises it; a declaration refused at `server.register(wf)` has no request to answer, and giving it a status would have meant inventing one. (3) **`Scope` is an alias of `Slot`, and `Placement` is a `Literal`.** 09 gives an action the same five words the slots use, and they coincide because they are the same question asked twice — a pane in the run slot is answered with a run. T049 names two enums, so `pane`/`card` is a two-value type rather than a third namespace nothing branches on. (4) **An action's model is read off its handler's `input` annotation** (`typing.get_type_hints`, because every module here carries `from __future__ import annotations`), with an explicit `model=` as the override. "The model **is** the form", so the type the author already wrote is the declaration; no `input` parameter means no input, and an `input` annotated with something that is not a pydantic model is a `TypeError` at declaration. (5) **`assets=` is stored unresolved beside the declaring module's directory**, captured from the constructing frame, because 09 resolves it relative to the module and not to the working directory. `validate(spec, graph, assets_root)`'s third parameter re-bases a *relative* declaration for a host that serves a package's data from elsewhere, and `PluginSpec.assets_dir(root)` is the one place the two are joined. (6) **A declaration after `finalize()` is refused**, exactly as `node()` is: the registry collects and validates against the finalized graph at registration, so one added afterwards is one no manifest, router or subscriber would ever see. (7) **`manifest_entry` lists the asset URLs and omits what does not apply.** 09's manifest shape carries `assets: [url]`, so the entry is complete rather than a placeholder T071 fills; T071 keeps the `StaticFiles` mount and the packaged-resources path. Absent fields are absent, not null (01 §Real data only), and the manifest route serializes with `exclude_none` so the wire matches the registry's own rendering. (8) **The plugin host is three tiers of its own** between `api` and `engine`: `mount` over `context` over the `engine`/`registry` tier, with `plugins.decl` at the bottom beside `graph`. T005's contract had all four modules as siblings of `engine`, which no arrangement of these imports can satisfy — `context` needs the engine's services and `mount` needs `context`. The **two** named arrows back up (`mount -> api.deps`, `mount -> api.openapi`) are the whole exemption: a plugin route is an operator route, and the door it hangs on is the API's. 02 §Layering and `AGENTS.md` now say the same. (9) **`_builtin` is spelled once**, in `athanore.plugins.registry.BUILTIN_WORKFLOW`. | **new (2026-09-07)** | (1), (2) and (6) are the same principle three times: 09's "errors at startup, never at runtime — the same contract the graph has". A panel pointing at a route nobody declared renders an empty card and logs nothing, which is the failure mode the check exists for, and it is a *check*, not a runtime refusal, so it needs an exception that is not about HTTP. (4) and (3) are both "do not make the author say it twice": the annotation and the slot are already there, and a second spelling is a second thing to keep in step. (5) is the one that would have been wrong quietly — a relative `assets=` resolved against the working directory works when the server is started from the workflow's own directory and breaks for every other caller, and the symptom is a manifest that lists nothing. (8) is the placement the imports force rather than one this task chose: the contract's placeholders were written before the modules existed, and the alternative to moving them was six ignore entries and a real cycle between `api.app` and `mount`. |
| D139 | The plugin mount, and the nine choices T049a made. (1) **`PluginError` is rendered by `athanore/api/errors.py` with the status the exception carries** — the one row of `DOMAIN_ERRORS` whose status is not the table's, beside `InvalidAnswer`'s extras. Without it a handler's considered 404 is a 500, and T070's action endpoint needs the same mapping. (2) **A service its scope cannot have raises on attribute access**, not on call, and there are two refusals: `no run in scope` (09's wording) and `no task in scope`. `log`, `stream`, `submissions` and `requests` belong to an *attempt*, and a run-scoped pane that reached for the work log would otherwise get a service scoped to a task id that is not there. (3) **`services.run` is a plugin-side object with `get()` and `list()`**, and `list()` is filtered to the plugin's own workflow — 09 has `services.run.list()` working in a `global` scope, and 12 §Plugins says a plugin cannot reach another workflow's runs, so an unfiltered list would have been the reach the 404 elsewhere prevents. The `_builtin` scope sees every run, because its panels apply to every run. (4) **`EventPort`'s `run_id` and `task_id` became optional**, so a `workflow`/`global` context can still publish `plugin.<workflow>.<name>`. `Event` already allows both to be absent; the port had simply only ever had one caller. (5) **The context is a generator dependency**, so whatever it opened is closed with the response: `services.stream` starts a background flusher on its first append, and a request-scoped one nobody closed would outlive the request. The dispatcher closes its contexts the same way. (6) **Ownership is a 404 in every direction**: a run of another workflow, a run or task that does not exist, a task of another run, a node the workflow does not have. Never a 403 — whether a run exists is not something one workflow's plugin learns about another's. (7) **`dispatch_handlers(bus, specs, host)` takes the host as a third argument** and is started and closed by the application's lifespan. One subscription, one consumer task, and the run's owner resolved once per event: from the store, or — when the row is gone, which `run.deleted` deterministically is, since the deletion commits before the bus sees it — from the event's own payload, which names the workflow (18 §Payloads). **Delivery is scoped by that owner in every case**: the owning workflow gets the event with no run in scope rather than every subscriber getting it, and an owner that cannot be established at all reaches only `_builtin`, which owns every run. A handler that raises is logged with its traceback and dropped; it cannot reach the engine at all, because the transaction committed before the bus published and this runs in the dispatcher's own task. Per-event delivery is wrapped the same way, because the owner lookup and the context's closing flush are store calls and one store error must not take the process's only consumer of the bus with it. (8) **`create_app(plugins=)` is `Sequence[PluginSpec]`**, and `WorkflowOut.plugin` is populated from the same `manifest_entry`, so 08 §Workflows' `plugin: {panels, actions}` stops being two empty lists the moment the registry lands. (9) **The action endpoint is not here.** `POST /api/plugins/{wf}/actions/{name}` is T070 and the `StaticFiles` assets mount is T071; this task mounts routes, the manifest and dispatch. | **new (2026-09-07)** | (2), (3) and (6) are one rule read from three sides: "handlers never get a partially-resolved context". A `None` where a run should be, an unfiltered list, or a 403 that confirms a run exists are each a way for a plugin to learn or touch something its scope does not cover, and each fails somewhere the author cannot place it. (1) and (5) are defects the mount would have shipped with: a `PluginError` from a context is raised on the *first* plugin route anyone writes, and the transcript flusher is the one service here with a background task behind it. (7) is the property the whole module exists to protect — a plugin cannot break the engine — and the three things that make it true are the commit ordering, the separate task, and the `except Exception` that logs. The deleted-run case is the one place delivery is deliberately degraded rather than refused: a subscriber to `run.deleted` asked precisely because the row is gone. Degraded in the *context*, not in the scope — the payload still names the owner, so ownership is decided rather than skipped, and a workflow never sees another's run id or title. |

(1) **A builtin reads through `PluginContext` like anything else**, which is what added `services.run.detail()`, `.log_entries()` and `.events()`: the overview needs a run's attempts and totals and the log pane needs the work log and the history, and a pane that draws a run is the most ordinary plugin there is. They are run-scoped and refuse with `no run in scope` exactly as `get()` does, so nothing widens. The alternative was a private path into the store for the core's own views, which would have made the builtins prove nothing. (2) **`detail()` is `RunRepo.detail`, the one read `GET /api/runs/{id}` makes**, so the overview's TOKENS and COST tiles and `RunDetail.stats` are the same numbers from the same query. (3) **The overview's route answers the `dashboard` shape plus a `meta` object.** 09 fixes `{note?, metrics, table?}` and 10 §Panes puts a `kv` grid between the tiles and the NODES table; a generic `dashboard` renderer draws the three it knows and ignores the fourth. Adding a key is what the declared kind allows — inventing a kind for one pane is not. Values are the raw facts, not formatted strings, so the renderer decides how to show them; POSITION is the one string, because "4 of 12" is two numbers read together and a tile shows one value. Metrics, table cells and `meta` entries are **absent** when nothing is known (01 §Real data only), so a run no agent has touched shows two tiles rather than four claiming zero. (4) **The log pane's `level` is 10's tone**, not a severity: the `log` kind gives a row one optional field to be coloured by and 10 names exactly three tones for this pane (`accent`, `dim`, `default`), so they are the same field. Events are merged with the work log on `created`, tie-broken entry-before-event, and each is rendered to one short sentence from its 18 payload — with the run's attempts by id in hand, so `task.enqueued` reads as the edge (`engineering → qa`) rather than as a task number. An event with no phrasing of its own falls back to its name, so a vocabulary that grows before this module does still renders. | D141 | The programmatic host, and the seven choices T051 made. (1) **The store, the bus, the request service and the engine are built in `Server.__init__`, the application and the socket in `start()`.** `register` has to reach the engine before anything is running, and a SQLAlchemy engine connects lazily, so constructing one touches no file: a `Server` that is never started opens no database. `stop()` disposes the *pool* rather than the engine, which is what lets one object start, stop and start again on the same store. (2) **`athanore/cli/verbs.py` is written here**, holding `RESERVED` and importing nothing, although 17 §T052 lists the file: T051's name check is its first reader, and the alternative — a second copy of the verb list in `server.py` — is the drift the single definition exists to prevent. T052 finds it made and adds the typer app, the client and the output beside it. Nothing may import the composition root; `Workflow.run` does, and defers it to call time (D96) so that the module an author defines a workflow in never pulls uvicorn, the API and the store in behind it. Without the exemption `grimp` reads that one line as `plugins.registry -> api`. (6) **Shutdown stops the engine first and the HTTP surface second** (04 §Shutdown): the attempts are cancelled and `engine.stopping` is written while the API can still answer, and the SSE streams close last. uvicorn is given `timeout_graceful_shutdown` of the same ten seconds `athanore.engine.SHUTDOWN_BUDGET` names, so the budget is one number. (7) **`start()` calls `configure_logging` and passes uvicorn `log_config=None`.** `athanore/logging.py` already anticipated it: uvicorn installs its own handlers from `Config.__init__`, so a programmatic host that did not undo them would put two shapes on stderr.
