| D149 | The Phase 3 checkpoint, and the four choices T056 made. (1) **Each of 13 §CI's three packages gets a gate of its own**, and T028's joint `graph`+`engine` step is split with them: measured off the full suite, `graph` is 100 %, `engine` 98 % and `requests` 99 %, so all three clear 95 % on their own. The overall gate is `coverage report --fail-under=85` with no `--include` — every file `--cov=athanore` measured, 93 % today. `tests/test_ci_workflows.py` grows the table those four steps are read against, so a gate that is deleted or renamed is a red test rather than a silent hole. (2) **`pyproject.toml` is not touched.** 17 §T056's file list names it, but nothing there is needed: `--cov=athanore` writes the data file and `coverage report --include= --fail-under=` reads it, and the one `[tool.coverage]` key that would change a number — an `exclude_also` for `if TYPE_CHECKING:` — could only inflate figures that already pass. (3) **The phase gate ran as a throwaway script**, per T041's precedent: a three-node workflow of `ACPAgent` seats on a real `Server`, `ATHANORE_AGENT_COMMAND` pointing every seat at `FakeACPAgent` and `ATHANORE_FAKE_SCENARIOS` selecting `gate.build.json` / `gate.review.json`, read back only through the API. `examples/` has no example workflows until T074, so there is nothing to commit it as. (4) **08 is corrected as well as extended.** 17 names three details to fold in — `state` precedence, the `_builtin` manifest entry, `payload_too_large` — and folding them in found five more places where 08 stated the design and Phase 3 shipped something else: `/api/health`'s three counts are optional, `tasks_in_progress` excludes `waiting`, `/api/me` reports `authenticated: true` under `auth: off`, an absent `X-Athanore-Token` is 422 rather than 403, and `?limit=` on `/events` and `/stream` has a documented default and cap. Those are corrections, not new scope. **No code changed**: the checkpoint reports defects, it does not fix them, and it found none to report. | **new (2026-09-07)** | (1) A joint threshold lets one package be carried by its neighbours — `graph` at 100 % would hide `engine` at 91 % — and the numbers say nothing has to be carried, so the stricter reading of 13 costs nothing and a red build names the package. (2) `AGENTS.md` §Quality bar's "no silently narrowed scope" cuts both ways: inventing a config block to make a file list come true is as dishonest as skipping one that was needed, and the honest deliverable is the row saying why. (3) is T041's answer to the same question, and the same one holds: the gate is a claim about the tree, not an artefact the tree should carry. (4) "08 and 09 accurate" is the exit condition, and a document that promises a field the code omits is worse than one that is merely silent — it is published into the generated client and read by the SPA. |
| D150 | The theme mapping, and the six choices T057 made. (1) **The status colours, the type scale and the two surfaces are `@utility` rules, not `@theme` entries.** Tailwind derives a `text-NAME` font-size utility from a `--text-NAME` entry, and `text-kicker` is a size *and* `text-transform: uppercase` *and* `letter-spacing: .12em` — one class cannot come from both places — so T007's `--text-*` block (D78) is replaced by six `@utility text-*` rules (`metric` 15 px/500, `body` 12, `row` 11.5, `secondary` 11, `kicker` 10.5, `hint` 10). D78's rule that `@theme inline` exposes only the shadcn roles is unchanged. (2) **Each status colour gets a `text-` and a `border-` utility and no `bg-`**: 10 §Status colours paints a label, a graph glyph and the outline of a `Badge variant="outline"`, never a fill. Seven, in the table's order; `paused` has no Nocturne token and takes `--color-accent-2-400`, 10's nearest role. `gen-theme.mjs` refuses to run if `nocturne.css` declares an `--ath-status-*` the list does not cover, so a status added to the source cannot reach the app as no utility at all. (3) **The keyframes are copied out of `nocturne.css`, not written**, and only `ath-pulse` and `ath-caret` are — `ath-scan` and `ath-flicker` went with the CRT chrome (D71). The timings sit on the utilities (`animate-ath-pulse` 1.8 s from 10 §Status colours, `animate-ath-caret` 1 s step-end from the mock), and the `prefers-reduced-motion` guard is **unlayered**, so it beats what Tailwind emits into `@layer utilities` whatever the source order. (4) **`pnpm gen:theme` writes a second file**, `web/src/styles/tokens.gen.ts`, parsed back out of the stylesheet the same run produced; `--check` covers both. `/__tokens` must list every token, and a component cannot read the stylesheet: Vite hands a `?raw` import of a `.css` file to the Tailwind plugin, which returns an empty string. (5) **`/__tokens` is reached from `main.tsx` by a dynamic `import()` behind `import.meta.env.DEV`**, since the router is T058's. `import.meta.env.DEV` is replaced with `false` at build time, so the branch and the module leave the production bundle. The utility class names on the page are literal strings, never assembled, because Tailwind emits a utility only when it finds the class as text in a source file — `Tokens.test.tsx` fails if the stylesheet grows a utility the page does not name. (6) **10 §Type and density is corrected**: its "Radius 2 px on everything" and "Glow: accent labels carry `text-shadow`" bullets are what D71 removed, and a later SPA task would have read them as normative. | **new (2026-09-07)** | (1) and (2) are the same question — what a class has to *do* — answered from the mock rather than from the shortest generator. A `text-kicker` that only set a size would be silently half a utility, and a `bg-status-ok` would be the first fill of a status colour anywhere in the design. (3) is the difference between a theme generated from the source and one transcribed from it: the two keyframes the app keeps are Nocturne's own bytes, and the two it drops are dropped by name, with a test that fails if either comes back. The guard ships with the animation because an accessibility pass that arrives later arrives after every screen has already used the class. (4) is the boring choice once the interesting one fails: the page has to enumerate tokens, the stylesheet is not readable from the browser, and a hand-written copy in the component would drift the first time `nocturne.css` changed — which is the one thing this task exists to prevent. (6) is `AGENTS.md`'s definition of done: the document that specifies the behaviour is updated when it changes, and D71 changed it without editing 10. |
| D151 | **The type scale's 11 px step is `text-meta`, not `text-secondary`, and `gen-theme.mjs` refuses any utility name Tailwind already derives from a `--color-*` role.** 10 §Tokens → shadcn maps `--secondary` onto `--color-surface`, so `@theme inline`'s `--color-secondary` gives Tailwind a `.text-secondary { color: var(--secondary) }`; a `@utility text-secondary` sets a size on that same class, both rules apply, and the class paints 11 px text #232532 on the #161826 page and beats a colour utility written beside it. `assertNoRoleCollision` now throws on such a name — for `text-`, `bg-`, `border-` and the other colour prefixes — and `theme.test.ts` compiles `src/index.css` with Tailwind's own `compile()` and asserts every generated utility paints exactly the properties its `@utility` declares. | **new (2026-09-07)** | The same one-name-two-sources failure D150(1) avoided for `text-kicker`, against a `--color-*` key instead of a `--text-*` one: the two halves of the generated stylesheet are written from different sections of 10 and nothing noticed the name appearing in both. The roles are shadcn's and cannot move, so the type scale gives way; `meta` is what the mock uses 11 px for (ids, chrome strips, meta lines). The static check states the rule and the compiled check is what actually sees the cascade — jsdom does not resolve Tailwind, so the suite was green with the text invisible on `/__tokens`. |
| D152 | The app shell, and the six choices T058 made. (1) **Search is validated in the router's `parseSearch`, not only in the route's `validateSearch`.** `validateSearch` alone does not keep an unrecognised key out of the app: a match's `search` — the field `useSearch`, `useLocation` and a `navigate({search})` updater's `prev` all read — is `{...parentSearch, ...validatedSearch}`, and the parent chain bottoms out in the raw parse, so a dropped key is merged straight back in underneath the validator (`?run=123` arrived as a number, `?run={"a":1}` as an object that React refuses to render). The SPA is one route, so the location's search and the app's search are the same object: validating at the parse puts the drop before the merge, and every match inherits it already validated. The route keeps `validateSearch` for the types it gives `Route.useSearch()`. The URL itself is left alone — a bookmark keeps whatever it carried, and the app never sees it. (2) **The validator drops per field and never throws**: `run` is any non-empty string (a run id is opaque; a run that does not exist is the API's 404, not a parse error), `pane` a zero-based integer index that the pane host clamps to the run's pane count in T062, `task` a positive integer because task ids are. Anything else is absent from the result. (3) **A path that is not `/` is replaced with `/`**, not shown as a not-found page: `mount_spa` answers every unrouted path with `index.html` (T047), so an old link reaches the SPA and the SPA is one route. (4) **`__APP_VERSION__` is read out of `pyproject.toml` by `vite.config.ts`** with a regex over the `[project]` table, and the build fails if the table or the key is missing. Node 22 has no TOML parser and a dependency to read one line would be the larger cost; the header must show the version of the distribution it was built into, so the one place it may come from is the one place that declares it. (5) **`useUi` holds one thing, the focus region (`list` | `detail`)**, and pointer-down or focus on either region sets it. It is neither linkable nor worth surviving a reload — it is where the next keystroke goes (T067) — which is the whole of the split against `usePrefs`. (6) **The footer's `^p palette` button sets `?overlay=palette`.** The overlay is T066a's; the state it opens from is this task's, so the button writes it rather than being drawn disabled. The chips read `D` for delete, per 10 §Keyboard, not the mock's `d`. | **new (2026-09-08)** | (1)-(3) are what makes a stale link open the app rather than white-screen it, which is the behaviour T058 names. (1) is where the first attempt was wrong, and jsdom could not see it: the test asserted on the match's private `_strictSearch`, which nothing in the app reads, so the suite was green while a real browser threw React error #31 on `?run={"a":1}`. The test now reads `match.search`, the field `useSearch` returns, and the rendered pane bar beside it. (4) keeps the header honest without a dependency. (5) keeps the two client stores from overlapping. (6) avoids both a dead button and a missing one. |
| D153 | The splitter and the list's collapse, and the six choices T058a made. (1) **The wide end is the detail pane's own 340 px minimum, not a maximum computed on the list.** `react-resizable-panels` caps a panel by its neighbour's constraints, so `window − 340` is stated once, in the pane it is about, and it then holds for a pointer drag, a resize key and a window resize with no code of ours in the path; the list's own constraint is its 260 px minimum and the width prefs remembers. The list is `preserve-pixel-size`, so a window that grows or shrinks moves the detail pane and leaves the list where the operator put it, until the 340 px takes over. The literal reading of 10 §Layout is 5 px looser than what ships: the handle is between the two panels, so the widest the list can be is `window − 340 − 5` and the detail pane keeps 340 exactly. (2) **A width is persisted only when the group reports `isUserInteraction`, and it is read from the layout, not from the DOM.** Every other source — the initial mount, a window resize, the group remounting when the rail expands — is left alone, so a width chosen on a wide window survives a spell on a narrow one; and `getSize().inPixels` is `offsetWidth`, which lags a resize key by a render (five `←` on a 1440 px window stored 280 for a list drawn at 260), where the layout percentage the callback is handed is already the new one. `usePrefs.setListWidth` clamps it again on the way to `localStorage`. (3) **The collapse toggle is in the pane bar and the rail is the only way back**, as in the mock: `❮` first in the bar, and collapsed, the rail's `❯` — never both, and never a control that would leave the operator with no list and no way to get one. Both write `listCollapsed`, which is where `b` binds in T067; the two titles name the key the way the footer chip already does. (4) **The rail reads `RUNS 0` while there are no rows**, like the list's `n shown` beneath it: it is the count of what is on screen, which is a number, not the unknown 02 §Real data only refuses to zero-fill. (5) **`src/test-setup.ts` gives jsdom a `ResizeObserver` that observes nothing**, because the library constructs one per mounted group and jsdom has none; and the splitter's own suite states the geometry it needs (`offsetLeft` and `offsetWidth` per panel id), because a library that works in pixels has no pixels in a DOM that never lays out. The two ends are asserted through the handle's `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuemax`, which are the constraints as the group resolved them. (6) **The chrome was driven in Chromium before it was called done**: the handle drags to 260 and to `window − 340`, `←` resizes and stops at the same two ends, the focus ring is the 2 px accent one of 10 §Accessibility, hover and drag light the bar accent-700 and accent-600 from the group's own `data-separator` rather than CSS `:hover` (which the 10 px hit region is wider than), the rail collapses and expands, and a reload into a narrower window clamps without losing the stored width. | **new (2026-09-08)** | (1) and (2) are the same question — who owns the pixels — answered so that only one place states each end. The alternative, a maximum recomputed on every window resize, is a listener and a re-render to express something the layout already knows, and it was the version that got the persisted width wrong. (3) and (4) are the mock and 02 read literally. (5) is the shape of every SPA test from here: jsdom resolves no cascade and no layout, so a test that needs either says so out loud, and (6) is there because it is the only thing that actually sees the result. |
| D154 | The API client bootstrap, and the six choices T059 made. (1) **The credential is withheld from a server that has said it does not want one, and from no other.** 17 §T059 says the `Authorization` header goes on when `/api/me.auth === "token"`, and read literally that never fires: the cache is empty when the app boots, so the handshake would go out bare, `authenticated` is a fact about *the request that asks* (08 §System, D129 (6)), and a browser holding a perfectly good token would meet the token screen on every reload. Sending it unless `auth` is known to be `"off"` keeps what the rule is for — no credential reaches a server that ignores credentials, from the first answer onwards — and costs one bearer header on the one request that is exempt from auth anyway. (2) **The auth mode is read out of the query cache, not mirrored into a store.** `/api/me`'s response is already cached under a generated key; a copy of `auth` in `useUi` would be a second one, and the interceptor would then be reading whichever of the two was updated last. (3) **`createAppQueryClient()` builds the query client and wires the fetch client to it in one call**, and clears the interceptor lists before filling them. The two are halves of one object — the credential comes out of that cache — so a query client that never met `configureApiClient` would send unauthenticated requests to a server that wants a token, and a second call replaces the wiring rather than stacking a second copy on it. (4) **`meQueryOptions()` is a function, not a constant.** The key the generator builds carries the client's `baseUrl`, so a constant evaluated at module load would carry whatever the config held before the client was configured, and the interceptor would read a cache entry nobody writes. (5) **`useMe` puts `queryFn` back explicitly.** `queryOptions` declares it optional and `tsconfig.app.json` sets `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, so the generated object is not assignable to `useQuery`'s parameter as it stands; the generator always sets it. (6) **`AppGate`'s stand-in for the token screen is a notice and a retry button**, not a form: T065 owns the form. It also renders the two states the gate cannot avoid having — still asking, and nothing answering — because a gate that showed a blank page when the server was down would be the SPA's first zero-fill. `src/test-setup.ts` gains a `Request` that resolves a relative URL against `window.location`, because jsdom implements no `fetch` and node's `Request` rejects the `baseUrl: ""` every deployment actually uses | **new (2026-09-08)** | (1) is the one place the task's wording and the task's exit condition pull apart, and the exit condition wins: "the shell loads unprompted" has to hold on a network bind with a stored token too, or the token screen is a loop. (2)–(4) are one instinct — there is exactly one copy of the server's answer and exactly one moment the client is configured — applied to the three places a second copy could creep in. (6) is 02 §Real data only read as a UI rule: "asking", "nothing there" and "needs a token" are three different facts and each gets its own sentence |
| D155 | The event feed, and the seven choices T060 made. (1) **The invalidation table's keys are the generated ones**, built by an `@hey-api` `*QueryKey` helper from the path parameters alone: 10 §Realtime writes `["run", e.run_id]`, but no query in this SPA is keyed that way (D49), so a literal transcription would invalidate nothing. A key built from the path and nothing else is a prefix, which is what lets `stream(4)` reach every page of task 4's transcript whatever `after` fetched it. (2) **`task.*` invalidates `runs` as well**, which the table's row does not say and the sentence under it does — "the header's active count and the run list come from `GET /api/runs`, refetched on `run.*`/`task.*`". 10's table is corrected to match its own prose. (3) **`registerRefreshOn` is consulted for every event name**, not only for `plugin.*`: 10 says panels register their `refresh_on` names "in the same table", and 09's own example registers `log.appended`, which is a core name. A panel's keys therefore join whatever row already matched. (4) **`appendStream` asks for everything after what the cache holds** — `min(held, seq_from - 1)` — rather than from the event's own cursor, and fetches nothing at all when no viewer holds the transcript. A cursor that ran ahead of a dropped frame would leave a hole nothing fills; a transcript nobody is looking at is fetched whole when it is opened. A page that cannot be merged, or a fetch that failed, falls back to invalidating the key. (5) **The feed's state is `useUi`'s**, written by the feed as the API client's 401 interceptor writes `needsToken` (D154) — it is the same kind of fact about this tab's conversation with the server, and the header and the banner read it from one place. (6) **`down` is two consecutive failures, not one.** A single dropped connection is ordinary and recovers inside a second; a banner on every one of them would be noise. `reconnecting` is therefore silent and `down` is what greys the counts and raises the banner. (7) **The feed reconnects at once when the stored token changes**, because the refusal that closed the stream may no longer apply and a 30 s backoff would otherwise leave the app deaf after a login that plainly worked. Two smaller ones: the runtime list of event names is the keys of a `Record<EventName, true>`, so a name added to the contract is a failed typecheck rather than a frame nobody hears; and `src/test-setup.ts` gains an inert `EventSource`, jsdom having none. | **new (2026-09-08)** | (1) and (2) are the same instinct: the table is normative about *which resources* an event touches, and the generated keys and the section's own prose are what it means by them. (3) is 09's example read literally — a panel that refreshes on `log.appended` is the documented case, and a registry consulted only for `plugin.*` would never fire for it. (4) is 02 §Real data only applied to a cursor: a hole in a transcript is worse than a redundant page, and a fetch nobody asked for is worse than both. (6) is the same rule as 02 §Real data only in the other direction — "the connection blinked" and "the server is gone" are different facts and only one of them is worth a banner |
| D156 | The run list, and the seven choices T061 made. (1) **The workflow chips and the `/` input live in `components/RunList/` and are rendered by `Header`.** 10 §Layout draws them on the header strip and 17 §T061 calls them the run list's; they narrow its rows and nothing else, so the module that owns the rows owns the controls and the strip only says where they go. (2) **Their state is `useUi`, not the URL and not a third store.** `src/routes/search.ts` validates exactly `run`, `pane`, `overlay` and `task` and drops the rest, so a filter cannot be a search parameter without widening 10 §Layout's list of what makes a view that view; `useUi` is already "client state deliberately not persisted and deliberately not in the URL" (D152). (3) **Filtering is client-side, over one cache entry.** `GET /api/runs` takes `?status` and `?workflow`, but a run list is small and returns whole (08 §Conventions), and a query key per chip would be a cache entry per chip where T060's invalidation table knows about one. (4) **The chips are the workflows there are runs of, sorted by name.** A chip for a registered workflow nobody has run would filter the list to nothing, and first-appearance order over a list in dispatch order would reshuffle the header every time a run was submitted or moved. (5) **`⚠` and the gate colour are one rule, and the gate row's treatment is plain.** 10 §Attention says a run with open requests gains the gate colour, so `statusTone` returns `gate` whatever the status is, and a `running` run parked on a question stops pulsing: nothing is in progress. (6) **`● k active` counts `running` and nothing else** (10 §Attention: "only while something is in progress"); `queued` and `paused` are not in progress, and before the server has answered both counts read `—` rather than `0` (02 §Real data only). (7) **The AGE column is re-rendered by a one-second clock in `useNow`.** It fetches nothing — freshness is still events only — but the column's finest unit is a second, so a row that only re-rendered on an event would show a number that is quietly wrong. The format is the mock's: `42s`, `4m`, `15.3h`, `16.7d`, and a timestamp that does not parse renders `—` rather than a guess. Two smaller ones: rows are `role="option"` buttons inside a `role="listbox"`, which is what `↑`/`↓` will move over in T067; and an App-level test types with `fireEvent` rather than `userEvent`, because jsdom performs no layout, every box is 0×0 at the origin, and the resizable group therefore reads a pointer press anywhere as a press on its handle and takes the focus. | **new (2026-09-08)** | (1), (2) and (3) all follow from there being one resource: the header's counts, the chips, the rows and the rail are four views of `GET /api/runs`, and anything that gave one of them its own copy — a second query key, a mirrored store, a server-side filter — would be a second copy that can be stale. (4) and (6) are 02 §Real data only: a control that cannot do anything and a count that is not a count are both worse than nothing. (5) is 10 read as one sentence rather than two rows. (7) is the one place the app is allowed a timer, and it is a clock rather than a poll |
| D157 | The pane host, and the five choices T062 made. (1) **The pane index lives in `?pane=` and is clamped where it is read, never rewritten.** 10 §Panes says it persists across selection changes and 10 §Keyboard says it clamps to the selected run's pane count; a host that wrote the clamped value back would truncate the operator's place the first time a run with fewer panes was selected and never give it back. (2) **`usePanes(runId, cursor)` is handed the index and the setter rather than reaching for the router.** Selection already works that way — `AppRoute` writes `?run=` — and the cycle's actions are also the keyboard's (T067), so the model is built once in `App` and handed to the bar: a dot, an arrow and a key all move one piece of state. (3) **A run's workflow comes from `GET /api/runs`, not from `GET /api/runs/{id}`.** The list is returned whole (08 §Conventions) and is already read for the rows, so scoping the manifest by ownership costs no request and cannot disagree with the row that was clicked; a run whose row has not arrived yet still gets the builtin panes, because `?run=` is a run. (4) **`GET /api/runs/{id}/graph` is fetched only where a `node`-slot pane is declared in scope.** Liveness is the only thing the pane host reads it for, the builtins declare no `node` panel, and the graph pane fetches it for itself (T063e). (5) **A type-scale utility and a colour utility are never passed through `cn` in one call.** `cn` is tailwind-merge and reads this theme's `text-row`, `text-hint` and `text-meta` (T057) as conflicting with `text-<colour>`, keeping only the last of the two: the scale goes on the container and is inherited, or the classes are concatenated plainly. That is a defect this task found in T061's run list, where the rows were rendering at the 12 px base instead of 11.5 px, and fixed. | **new (2026-09-08)** | (1), (2) and (3) are all one idea: the pane cycle is derived from the manifest and the selection, and nothing about it is stored twice. (4) is the cost of freshness kept honest — one request, and only where liveness decides a pane. (5) is a trap every later frontend task would otherwise fall into |
| D158 | **A panel's `source` is asked with the parameters its declared `scope` names, and a panel whose scope is not resolved is not asked at all.** A `run`-scoped panel is fetched with `run_id`, a `node`-scoped one with `run_id` and its own `node`, a `task`-scoped one with `task_id`, and a `workflow`/`global` one with nothing; the pane says what it is waiting for instead of firing a request with a missing id. Its cache key is `['panel', <source>, <params>]`, and the `['panel', <source>]` prefix — which TanStack matches partially — is what `refresh_on` is registered against at manifest load | **new (2026-09-08)** | 09 §Context and scopes makes every missing or mismatched id a 404 *before* the handler runs, so a request without them can only fail; and sending every id the SPA happens to know would put `?task=` in the key of a `run` panel, refetching the overview each time the task drawer opened. Registering the prefix is what lets one registration, made before any run is selected, reach every run's copy — the same property the generated keys have (D155) |
| D159 | **`refresh_on` names are matched against an event's name as globs, and `task.stream` reaches only a panel that registered it by that exact name** | **new (2026-09-08)** | 09 §Builtins are plugins declares the log pane "refresh on `log.appended` and `task.*`", and every builtin's `refresh_on` carries a glob — the overview's `run.*` and `task.*` — so an exact-only lookup left those panes never refreshing (T062a found this). 09 §Wire contract said nothing about how the names are matched; this task added the sentence that does, so the manifest's field carries its semantics where the field is specified. The `task.stream` exception is 10 §Realtime's own rule one level down: two or three of those arrive per second per streaming task, and a `task.*` panel refetching at that rate is exactly what first-exact-then-glob exists to prevent |
| D160 | **Markdown code fences are highlighted by shiki loaded lazily from its `bundle/web` on the JavaScript RegExp engine, themed `github-dark-default` with its background dropped**; a fence naming a language the bundle does not carry stays the plain `<pre><code>` | **new (2026-09-08)** | The engine is forced: shiki's default oniguruma is WebAssembly and the SPA is served under `script-src 'self'` with no `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` (12 §Plugins), so the module loads, the instantiation is refused, and every block silently stays plain — relaxing the policy for a highlighter is not a trade worth making. The bundle is a size choice: the full one is 300 chunks and 10 MB of wheel for grammars nothing here writes, and the web one carries Python, shell, JSON, YAML, SQL, TypeScript and markdown. The theme is one of shiki's own because Nocturne defines no code-token palette (10 §Design system stops at the app's chrome) |
| D161 | **The overview pane formats a value by the field it is, not by its type**: TOKENS and AGENTS grouped (`612,884`), COST as `$0.2914`, DURATION and the NODES `DUR` column in whole seconds (`246s`, one decimal below ten), AGE in the run list's humanised shorthand (`4m`, `15.8d`), SESSION cut to its first 8 characters. A field the route omitted is omitted — except DESCRIPTION, which keeps its row and reads `—` — and a field this build has no rule for is printed by the generic formatter | **new (2026-09-08)** | The route sends raw counts and seconds (`overview.py`) and the generic `formatValue` prints a number as the number, which would read the cost as `0.29` and the age as `246.4`. The formats are the design mock's own (`docs/v1/design/Athanore.dc.html`: `612,884`, `$0.2914`, `246s`, `4m`), so this is the mock's copy applied to the route's data rather than a scheme invented here. 10 §Panes fixes only two of them — AGE "humanised" and SESSION "shortened to 8 characters" — and is silent on the rest. A registry the server answered empty is a notice and a `cancel` button, not a form with nothing to submit. (3) **A queued run is put back in view**: the run list is invalidated and the header's workflow chip and `/` box are cleared, as the mock's `submitNewRun` clears them. Restating the defaults is the boring choice and the one pyright's own documentation implies; the alternatives were worse: git-ignoring `.venv` harder does nothing (pyright does not read `.gitignore`), and naming `.venv` alone would leave the same trap set for the next dot-directory. | D174 | **Five things T066d decides about the edit overlay and the four pickers.** (1) **A picker offers only what the op would accept.** 04 §Operator operations gives each of the four a precondition and the server enforces it, but a list that offers a row the server is going to refuse teaches the rule by refusing the operator — so `t` lists the attempts that have *stopped* (`Ops.retry` refuses `ready`, `in_progress` and `waiting`), `x` lists the ones still going, `m` lists every attempt and **never a join as the target** (04 §Fan-in, `409`), and `r` lists every node *including* joins, because a join rerun replays the arrivals the store holds. (2) **`cancel task` is restricted although `set_status` is not.** The op takes any task; the list does not, because cancelling an attempt that already finished would overwrite the status it earned with `cancelled` and lose what happened. (3) **Attempts are listed newest first**, against the wire's oldest-first timeline order: a picker is not a timeline, and the attempt an operator reaches for is the one that just stopped. (4) **A refusal keeps the panel up and a success closes it.** A `409` is corrected by picking another row, so the message is drawn on the panel beside the rows; a call that landed has changed the run and reports on the toast surface. Both are latched one-at-a-time, taken synchronously, for the reason D171 (1) gives. A picker with nothing eligible draws the sentence naming the precondition rather than an input over an empty box. Each picker is a list plus one POST" and "Move must not offer a join node". (1) is that fence taken past the one case the plan names: the plan says a join is "an error you can only discover by trying", and the same sentence is true of every other precondition in the table, so the rule is applied once rather than four times inconsistently. (2) is the only one of the four the docs leave open — `set_status` has no precondition — and 01 §Real data only decides it: a `done` attempt overwritten with `cancelled` is a record of what happened replaced by a record of what did not. | D180 | **The Phase 4 packaging check is `scripts/check_wheel.py`, it runs `uv build` itself, and `./scripts/test.sh` runs it.** (1) It is a script and not a block of shell in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, like `scripts/dump_openapi.py` before it. | D181 | **Six things T070 decides about running an action.** (1) **The endpoint is one route for the process, not one per workflow**: `POST /api/plugins/{wf}/actions/{name}` is mounted by `mount_actions(app)` beside the manifest and before the per-workflow routers, so it is in the committed OpenAPI snapshot and a workflow declaring a route at `/actions/...` cannot shadow it. (3) **Everything else this endpoint refuses is a `PluginError`**, and therefore `code: "plugin_error"` with the status the refusal means: an unregistered workflow and an undeclared action are 404s like the ownership 404s the context already raises (D139 (6)), which keeps one code for the whole plugin surface. (4) **The action's declared `scope` is checked before the handler runs**, in the wording its services would have used — `no run in scope`, `no task in scope`, and a third of the family, `no node in scope` — because "handlers never get a partially-resolved context" (09) is a promise about the handler, not about the first service it happens to touch. (6) **A palette row runs the action, through an overlay of its own** (`?overlay=action&action=<workflow>:<name>`): `overlays/actions.ts` says the palette "lists what the app can do and nothing else", so a row that only listed would be the stub the quality bar forbids — and an action is a form, which needs a surface. `components/ActionRunner.tsx` is that form, the confirm dialog and the POST, and it is the same component the `form` panel kind mounts. (2) avoids a third arrow from `plugins.mount` up into `athanore.api` (D138 names two and calls them the only ones) and gets the better answer anyway: `validation_error_handler` already projects `loc`/`msg`/`type`, and the SPA needs the paths relative to the model rather than to the request body. (3) is consistency with what the same request already answers for a run of another workflow; the status is what carries the meaning, and one code for `/api/plugins/...` is one branch for the client. (4) is the difference between a refusal an author can place and one they cannot: a `run`-scoped action invoked with no run would otherwise fail wherever the body first reached for `ctx.run`, or silently do the wrong thing if it reached for nothing. `NO_NODE` is new because no service is scoped to a node alone, so nothing had needed to say it before. (5) is the only reading the manifest supports — there is no other title — and it is the one T066a's own tests were already written against (`plugin: one`, `plugin: two`). (6) is 10 §Overlays' "every operator action is listed with its key" taken at its word, plus D174 (4)'s split: a refusal keeps the panel up and a success closes it. | D182 | **`script-src` gains `'unsafe-eval'`, and without it no form in the SPA can be submitted.** RJSF validates with ajv8 (02 §Library choices), and ajv compiles every schema — the operator's form, and the JSON Schema meta-schema it checks that form's schema against — into a `new Function`. Under the `script-src 'self'` of D136 the browser refuses that call, ajv throws where it compiles, RJSF logs "Form validation failed" and calls nothing. The policy is therefore `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-eval'`, with `'unsafe-inline'` still refused for scripts and `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` still refused entirely (D160). 12 §Plugins and 09 §Security carry the new string and the reason; `tests/api/test_static.py` asserts the directive on its own, beside the whole-policy comparison, so the two cannot be corrected back the other way by somebody reading 12 and not the browser. | **new (2026-09-09)** | Found by opening T070's `form` pane in Chromium against `./scripts/run.sh`: the pane drew, the fields drew, and `submit` did nothing at all. It is not T070's defect — it has been true of every `form` request and every elicitation since T064, which is to say of every RJSF form the served application has ever drawn — and it is invisible to the suite that covers them, because jsdom evaluates under no content policy. T070 is where it had to be fixed, since "the `form` panel kind renders `ActionForm(schema)`" is not delivered by a form that cannot be submitted. The alternative was a validator that does not compile code, which means replacing `@rjsf/validator-ajv8` with an adapter written here over an interpreting library — a stack change and a new dependency, for a directive whose actual reach is code built from strings by scripts that already loaded. D160 turned the highlighter's `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` down because highlighting degrades to plain text; a form that submits nothing degrades to an application the operator cannot answer a question in |
| D183 | **Six things T071 decides about assets, `custom` panels and `window.athanore`.** (1) **A relative `assets=` is resolved against the declaring module's directory first and `importlib.resources.files(<its package>)` second**, and only when the first holds no such name — which is the installed case, where the data a wheel ships need not sit beside the module that declared the workflow. `__file__` is exact where it exists, so it is not second-guessed; a host's explicit `assets_root` overrides both; a directory none of them finds is `PluginValidationError` at `server.register(wf)`, naming the path *and* the package it also looked in. (2) **`window.athanore.fetch` may not leave `/api/plugins/{wf}/`**: a relative path is resolved under that prefix and `..`, an absolute URL and a protocol-relative path are a `TypeError` before a request is made. "Bound to" is a boundary or it is a base URL, and 09's "Nothing else" makes it the first. (3) **The global is installed once and the element carries the same object.** `fetch` needs a workflow and `window` is one object, so `window.athanore` is bound to the workflow whose element mounted most recently — which is the only one there is on every screen of every deployment with one plugin that ships assets — and `CustomElementHost` also sets `athanore` on the element itself, bound to that element's own workflow. Same three capabilities; only where they are reached from differs. **Both are in place before the element is inserted into the document**, which is why the host builds the element and appends it rather than rendering the tag as JSX: an already defined tag runs `connectedCallback` synchronously at insertion, and React inserts a host node before it attaches a ref and long before it runs an effect — a bridge handed over from either would be there on a document's first mount, where the tag is still undefined and the upgrade deferred, and missing on every mount after it. (4) **`subscribe(names, cb)` matches globs and swallows nothing.** The names are matched the way `refresh_on`'s are (`eventNameMatches`, one matcher for all three callers), each is `watch`ed on the tab's one `EventSource` so a plugin's own `plugin.*` name arrives, a subscriber that throws is logged and dropped, and a document with no feed open gets an `Error` rather than a subscription to nothing. (5) **A missing plugin asset answers the API's error shape.** `PluginAssets` rewrites `StaticFiles`' `{"detail": "Not Found"}` into the `{error, code: "not_found"}` of 08 §Conventions, so a URL under `/plugins/…` that names no file answers one way whether or not the workflow that would have served it ships any assets — the unmounted case already answered that shape, through `SPA`'s reserved-prefix branch. Only a 404 is rewritten. (6) **The element table is one table.** `web/src/plugins/registry.ts` maps a tag to a renderer, the three the core ships register into it at module load, and a tag with no entry goes to `CustomElementHost` to be mounted for real — so the host has no branch that a plugin's tag could never reach. A tag whose workflow ships no assets, and which nothing else has defined, draws the placeholder card rather than an empty element | **new (2026-09-09)** | (1) 09 §Escape hatch says "resolved relative to the module; package data when installed" and 17 §T071 says "`importlib.resources` when packaged": two answers, so the order between them had to be chosen, and the one that keeps every existing checkout working is the module first. (2) and (3) are what 09's block does not say and a second plugin makes visible; the element property is the only unambiguous answer available, and it adds no capability to the three — and owning the insertion is the only way either bridge is true of the second mount as well as the first, which is every time an operator cycles back to the pane. (4) follows 09 §Wire contract's globs and 09 §Mounting's "a plugin cannot break the engine", read on the browser's side. (5) is 01 §Real data only applied to a refusal: two shapes for one question is a client branch nobody asked for. (6) is 09 §Builtins are plugins' "the host page has no hard-coded knowledge of them" — a lookup only the core's tags could reach would be that knowledge under another name |
| D184 | **Four things T072 decides about entry-point discovery.** (1) A **broken entry point stops the server**: a module that will not import, an attribute that is not there, a value that is not a `Workflow` or a factory that raised is a `DiscoveryError`, which `athanore serve` prints as a sentence naming the entry, its distribution and the cause, and exits 1 — not 2, because nothing was mistyped. `--no-discover` is the documented way past a package that cannot be fixed today. (2) Precedence is applied to the **workflow's own name**, not the entry point's: the left-hand side of an entry is what the distribution called it, and the name a run is submitted under, that `[workflows]` binds and that `Server.register` refuses a duplicate of, is the `Workflow`'s. (3) Entries are loaded in a **fixed order** — entry-point name, then value — so two installations of the same packages register the same workflows in the same order, and two distributions advertising one workflow name are refused by the registration identically every time rather than resolved by whichever the file system yielded first. (4) `athanore.plugins.discovery` is **not added to the import-linter layers**: it imports the standard library and `athanore.workflow` and nothing else, which is exactly where `workflow.py` itself sits — outside the tiers, joining `graph` and `plugins.decl` — so a new tier entry would have had to name a module 02 §Layering does not list | **new (2026-09-09)** | (1) is 01 §Real data only applied to an absence: skipping the entry would start a server whose missing workflow is next observed as a 404 on a name that is definitely installed, and the operator cannot fix what they were not told about. It is the same refusal 17 §T072 already fixes for an unknown pool name, at the one other place discovery can be silently wrong. (2) is forced by 09 §Discovery's own example, where the entry name and the workflow name are equal and nothing makes them so: the engine, the API and `athanore.toml` know only one of the two. (3) is what makes the collision in (2) a property of the installation rather than of the disk. (4) keeps `pyproject.toml`'s contract a transcription of 02's diagram — a layer this task invented would have put the two out of step, and AGENTS.md quotes the same diagram a third time |

The fixture therefore has one route per data kind, plus the `POST` a custom element calls — which is also where `methods=` is exercised. (2) **`fixture_workflow(name)` is a factory, and every `plugin.*` name it publishes is derived from that name.** A second copy under another name is what "a foreign run" is asserted against, and a plugin may publish only in its own namespace (18 §Plugins), so a hard-coded `plugin.gamedev.*` in a workflow built as `other` would be a fixture that cannot run. (3) **The `on` handler proves "after commit" by re-reading the store**, not by inspecting the event payload: it publishes the status and output `services.run.get()` returned, so the assertion is that the run was already durable when the handler ran. | **new (2026-09-09)** | 17 §T073 fixes what the fixture declares and what the suite asserts, and leaves the shape of the fixture itself open. Each of the three is the boring choice made explicit: the fixture is the reference example a plugin author reads (the task plan says so), so it may not model its panels on a route that returns the wrong shape, may not carry a namespace it does not own, and may not assert a timing property by restating the payload it was handed. |

The name survives in `docs/v1/design/Athanore.dc.html`, whose sample transcript reads `artificer/tui.py`, and in `web/src/panes/__tests__/fixtures.ts`, which transcribes that line: the mock is the reference for layout and copy (AGENTS.md §Conventions), so editing either would make the fixture diverge from what it is a transcription of. | **new (2026-09-09)** | A task that says "verify" is not licence to reverse a decision the log already carries: D148 chose per-access with the reason still standing (the alias is cached under the canonical name only, so a once-per-process warning is silent for every importer after the first — and Python's default filter already collapses the repeat to one line per call site), so the honest reading of "once" is the one that leaves the shim useful, with the ambiguity recorded here rather than resolved silently in either direction. The sweep is bounded the way the plan bounds it — the rows of 15 from D59, each read against the document it names — so it is a finite, checkable list rather than a re-read of twenty documents; what it found sound is worth recording too: 08 covers all 34 paths of the OpenAPI snapshot, all 19 `ErrorCode` members, and the CLI surface of 11 is the verbs `athanore --help` lists.
