| D191 | **Six things T079 decides about the audits and the release.** (1) **Both audits are CI's and not the gate's, which is the one place D74 and D178 do not reach.** Every other check in `ci.yml` is a check `./scripts/test.sh` runs, because this repository has no runner and a CI-only check would never run at all; `pip-audit` and `pnpm audit` are the exception, because their verdict comes from an advisory database rather than from the tree. A gate that answers "is this branch green" must not change its answer overnight without a commit, and it must not need the network. They run **last** in their job for the same reason: an advisory published overnight must not stop `pytest`, `ruff`, `pyright`, the E2E suite or the packaging check from running first. (2) **The one high advisory found is fixed, not waived.** `pnpm audit --audit-level high` reported three high advisories against `js-yaml` 4.2.0 (GHSA-52cp-r559-cp3m, GHSA-5p4m-2wfm-xmqj, GHSA-2883-xcg3-v3hh — quadratic CPU on merge-key chains and on `!!omap`), reached only through `@hey-api/openapi-ts` → `@hey-api/json-schema-ref-parser`. `pnpm-workspace.yaml` grows an `overrides` entry, `"js-yaml@<4.3.2": "^4.3.2"`, which dedupes onto the 4.3.2 the rest of the tree already resolved; the generated client is byte-identical afterwards. Bumping the pinned `openapi-ts` instead would have regenerated committed code on the release commit. `pip-audit` is clean (it skips `athanore` and `athanore-examples` themselves, which are not on PyPI at this version). (3) **"For real" is two closures, not one.** The nightly job could be green having tested nothing in two ways, and both are shut: the `pytest_sessionfinish` carve-out of D76 that rewrote exit 5 to 0 for a `-m` selection is deleted with `tests/conftest.py`, now that T014's tests make the selection non-empty; and `ATHANORE_TEST_PG_REQUIRED`, set by the job, turns `tests/store/conftest.py`'s two Postgres skips (no URL, nothing listening) into failures. The skip itself stays, because it is right on a dev machine that has not run `docker compose --profile pg up -d postgres`. Anything but empty / `0` / `false` / `no` arms it, so a typo in a workflow file cannot silently disarm it. (4) **The coverage gates needed no change: they were already enforced in T056, and this task is where they were first *run*.** graph 100 %, engine 98 %, requests 99 %, `athanore` overall 93 % — against thresholds of 95/95/95/85. Running `pytest -m postgres` against a live database is the one verification step of `docs/plans/T079-release.md` this task did **not** perform: the container it was built in is an agent container and mounts no docker socket, so the `pg` profile could not be started from it. Both no-database postures were exercised instead (unset skips; `ATHANORE_TEST_PG_REQUIRED=1` errors), and the live path is not one this task touches. (5) **The tag is local and nothing is published.** There is still no git remote (T006, T008, D113), so `v1.0.0` is `git tag` in this checkout and the "push" of 17 §T079 is not done rather than done against an invented remote. PyPI is untouched, and the README says so instead of claiming a release. `examples/pyproject.toml` is bumped with `pyproject.toml`: they are one workspace and one release. (6) **The clean-machine check is the two wheels, not one.** T079's **Done** wants the SPA served and `msgtest` run, and `msgtest` is an entry point of `athanore-examples`; `uv build --all-packages` and `pip install` of both into a fresh venv is what a machine with no checkout has. What was observed is in the task report and in 17 §T079's status line. | **new (2026-09-09)** | An audit is the one check whose result is a function of the date, so binding the gate to it would make "green" mean "green today", and a red gate that no commit caused is how a team learns to ignore the gate. Fixing the advisory in the lockfile rather than waiving it keeps the check meaningful and costs one line, and bounding the override below (`@<4.3.2`) means the entry stops applying once the dependency catches up rather than pinning a version nobody asks for any more. A nightly job that provisions a database and then skips because it could not reach it is worse than no job: it reports green for the exact failure it exists to catch, and it does so silently for as long as nobody reads the log. D76's carve-out was correct while the suite it guarded did not exist and is a hole in the guard now that it does — which is precisely the kind of scaffolding a release is the last chance to take down. Tagging locally and saying so is the honest version of "tag, push"; inventing a remote to push to would make the release step untrue in the one document a reader trusts. |
| D192 | **Seven things the `planner` seat decides.** (1) **It is a second workflow over the same working tree, not a stage of `feature`.** `feature` builds one task and is told which one; deciding what the tasks are ends in a human gate, and a `feature` run that asked a person to approve a design before implementing would gate every task on a question that belongs to the whole plan. (2) **`verify` is `feature`'s gate, for a branch a test suite cannot judge.** `./scripts/test.sh` has nothing to say about a document, so the deterministic node reads `git diff --name-only` instead: the design document and every plan file must be files the branch actually wrote, the task ids must be free, and nothing outside `docs/` may be touched. An architect that described files it did not write would otherwise pass the human gate — a person reading a proposal cannot see an absent file — and fail at the far end as an implementer with no plan to follow. (3) **A task id is taken if any spent id starts with it**, because `plan_docs` resolves a plan by globbing `docs/plans/<id>*.md`; the ids already spent are read at the merge base from that directory and from the `### Txxx` headings of `17-serial-task-plan.md`, so a proposal is not counted as a collision with itself. (4) **`adopt` merges before `dispatch` queues.** `feature` branches from `main` and resolves a task's plan from the working tree at the moment its implementer needs it, so queued first, the first implementer would branch from a `main` with no plan on it. (5) **The `feature` runs are submitted over the HTTP API**, not through the engine: `TaskServices` deliberately carries no `ops` (04 §TaskContext), and 02's one wire contract already has the verb — `POST /api/workflows/feature/runs`, the same call the CLI and the SPA make. The operator token comes from `.athanore/token` when the bind is not loopback, read as a file for the same reason the seats read git as a subprocess. (6) **Both seats run Fable through `agent-claude-fable`, not through a `model` on the default service.** `claude-agent-acp` refuses `session/set_config_option` for anything outside its menu plus `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` (D75), and a rejected option is a warning rather than a failure (D11), so the session's model is read from `BUILDER_REVIEW_MODEL` — the one variable `compose.yaml` sets that service's environment from — and cannot drift from it. (7) **One capacity-1 `checkout` pool for both seats, and `adopt` refuses a dirty tree.** There is one working tree, so there is one cap; but a `human_input` gives its slot back for the duration of the wait (04 §Waiting), so a `feature` run queued behind an unanswered `approve` will start and branch. Merging over somebody else's uncommitted work is the one outcome that loses it, so `adopt` raises instead — unlike `feature`'s `merge`, which discards post-gate scratch it knows QA made. | **superseded by D202** | The gate is the definition of green and a plan has no gate, so the property `feature` is built to have — no agent rules on its own work — needed a deterministic equivalent or the human gate would be the only check on a document nobody had read back |
| D193 | **The implementer runs narrow checks, not the gate.** `ImplementerAgent`'s prompt told it that running `./scripts/test.sh` itself was the fastest way to avoid a red gate; it now tells it not to, and names the targeted commands instead — `pytest` on the tests it touched, `ruff` and `pyright` on the paths it changed, `lint-imports` only when an import moved, `pnpm -C web` only when `web/` changed. The full gate stays exactly where it was: the `gate` node, once, on the branch | **new (2026-09-09)** | The gate is ruff, pyright, import-linter, both suites, the SPA build, Playwright and the packaging check — many minutes, and the `gate` node already runs it for real on the same branch. Paying it again inside every implement attempt, and again on every loop-back, spends the run's wall clock on a verdict that is about to be taken properly anyway; the narrow checks catch the same faults in the code the agent actually wrote |
| D194 | **The mobile layout is the stacked split on one breakpoint.** Below Tailwind's `md` (768 px) the splitter is not mounted and the SPA shows one middle region at a time: the run list while `?run=` is unset, the detail pane while it is set; a back control in the pane bar clears it. `listWidth` and `listCollapsed` are inert below the breakpoint, kept rather than cleared. The keyboard map stays fully bound at every width; narrow adds touch routes and removes none (21 §Narrow layout) | **new (2026-09-09)** | The design import defines no mobile layout, so one is decided here. `?run=` already is the selection (D152, D179), so stacked list↔detail navigation adds no new state, stays linkable, and needs no second router; the 260 px + 340 px splitter minimums cannot coexist below ~600 px anyway |
| D195 | **The type ramp becomes base-relative; density does not scale.** D150's six type utilities keep their names and treatments but their sizes become `calc(<px>rem / 12)` — exact fractions of the mock's 12 px base — so at the default base every utility resolves to the same pixels as before, and a changed `<html>` font-size rescales metric values, rows, kickers and hints together. The chooser's four steps are multipliers of `--ath-font-size` (`small` ×11/12, `default` ×1, `large` ×13.5/12, `xlarge` ×15/12), applied as a `data-font-size` attribute on `<html>` that the generated theme maps; spacing, paddings and the mock's pixel chrome stay absolute (21 §Type scale) | **new (2026-09-09)** | Absolute px was right when the base was fixed; a chooser makes it wrong — six utilities that ignore the base would produce mixed-scale text at every non-default step. Multiplying the token rather than hard-coding px lets a re-imported base flow through the steps unedited. Scaling density too would break the mock's pixel-specified layout for no stated need |
| D196 | **The font-size control is a header popover plus palette rows, persisted in `usePrefs.fontSize`.** An icon-only text-size button beside `workflows` opens a Radix popover with a radio group of the four steps; the command palette lists them as rows. The pref persists in the `athanore.prefs` store, so clearing site data restores the default. No settings overlay is created; the SPA still has none | **new (2026-09-09)** | `usePrefs` is the established seam for what is personal to a browser (10 §Layout), and the header is the one always-visible chrome in both layouts, so the control is reachable by touch on narrow and by `^p` on desktop without inventing a settings surface for one radio group |
| D197 | **The mobile gate is one pinned viewport, 390×844, touch-only, plus axe at both layouts and at `xlarge`.** The mobile Playwright spec runs at 390×844 with `hasTouch` and `isMobile` and activates everything with `tap()`; the axe gate (D178's floor) runs at the desktop viewport, at 390×844, and at the desktop viewport with `xlarge` selected. 360 px is covered by 21's fluid no-horizontal-scroll rule, not by a second e2e viewport. D204 (5) adds a fifth state at the desktop viewport — the run list with a `failed` run held by `⏎` — for the reason given there | **new (2026-09-09)** | One iPhone-class viewport exercises every narrow code path; a 360 px twin would re-run the same assertions against the same fluid CSS. `tap()` is what proves touch-alone operation — a `click()` passing says nothing about it |
| D198 | **The re-import copies verbatim and stops on structural change.** T080 refreshes `docs/v1/design/` byte-for-byte from the Claude Design project and regenerates the theme; it MUST stop and ask rather than reconcile silently when the refreshed artifacts add or remove an `--ath-status-*` name, add a theme or mode, change token families rather than values, or prescribe a mobile layout or font-size control that differs from 21 — those supersede 21 only through the operator's ruling and a decision row. It is blocked until the operator authorizes DesignSync (`/design-login`, once, interactively) or places the refreshed files in `docs/v1/design/` | **new (2026-09-09)** | The mock is the reference (AGENTS.md §Conventions) but 21 is the approved design; when the two disagree the operator rules, per the quality bar's "if the specs are ambiguous, ask". The verbatim rule keeps the import reviewable as a plain diff |
| D199 | **The generated theme pins Tailwind's `--spacing` to 3 px, so the font-size chooser scales type and not density.** Tailwind v4's spacing scale is `--spacing: 0.25rem` a step, and every `gap-*`, `p*-*`, `w-*` and `h-*` utility in the SPA is a multiple of it; with the ramp of D195 made relative to `<html>`, that scale would grow with the base the chooser sets and take every padding and gap in the app with it. `@theme inline` now declares `--spacing: 3px` — exactly what `0.25rem` already resolves to at the mock's 12 px base, so the default rendering is unchanged to the pixel — and no component's spacing was converted either way | **new (2026-09-09)** | D195 and 21 §Type scale say density stays absolute, and until this the sentence was only true of the arbitrary pixel values the mock specifies: the handful of Tailwind scale utilities the app does use (`left-1`, `top-1`, `gap-y-2`, the vendored table's `px-2`) would have quietly grown by a quarter at `xlarge`. Pinning the scale is one line in the generator and says it once, where converting four call sites to arbitrary pixels would have said it four times and left the fifth to be found later. 3 px rather than Tailwind's nominal 4 px because the base here is 12 px, not 16: the value that changes nothing is the one that is already on screen |
| D200 | **The 2026-09-09 re-import changed nothing, and the project's second mock is imported but not adopted.** T080 re-imported `docs/v1/design/` over DesignSync: `Athanore.dc.html` came back byte-identical, and the Nocturne token sheet (`_ds/nocturne-…/styles.css`, enumerated by `_ds_manifest.json`) still declares exactly the 51 tokens `nocturne.css` records, at the same values — no `--ath-status-*` added or removed, no second theme or mode (`"themes": []`), no token family changed. The old→new `nocturne.css` diff is therefore empty, `gen:theme` is a no-op, and 10 §Design system needed no fold. What the project *has* grown is a second mock, `Athanore Terminal.dc.html`, imported verbatim beside the first and **normative for nothing**. The app does not follow it, in three parts: (1) **its chrome** — `border-radius: 0` everywhere in place of Nocturne's 8 px, chrome borders moved from `--color-neutral-800/900` to `--color-accent-800/900`, and ASCII framing (`┤ ATHANORE ├`, `« runs`, `<-`, a `$` palette prompt, a cursor column on the selected run) — because restyling components is fenced out of T080 by `docs/plans/T080-design-reimport.md` §What this task is not, and because it is a look, not a token: not one value in the token sheet moved. (2) **Its text-size control** — five steps (`compact` .9, `default` 1, `large` 1.15, `x-large` 1.3, `huge` 1.5) applied as CSS `zoom` on the root, driven by an `a− / A+` stepper in the header — which differs from 21 §Type scale in every respect that matters: `zoom` scales density with type, which D195 rejected on purpose, and T081 has already shipped the four-step rem ramp and its popover. (3) **Its narrow layout** — a `matchMedia('(max-width: 760px)')` breakpoint and a `mobileView: 'list' | 'detail'` state — which differs from 21 §Narrow layout's `md` (768 px) and its `?run=`-carried stacking (D194). (2) and (3) are D198 stop-and-ask triggers, so 21 stands and T082 builds to 21 until the operator rules otherwise; that ruling, if it comes, supersedes 21 here and in its own row | **new (2026-09-09)** | D198 says the operator rules when the mock and 21 disagree, and this mock disagrees with 21 on the two things 21 was written to decide — after 21 chose differently in full view of them (D195's rationale is an argument against scaling density, which is precisely what `zoom` does). Importing the file verbatim rather than describing it keeps that ruling reviewable as a plain diff, which is D198's own reason for the verbatim rule; leaving it out would have made the operator re-fetch the thing they are ruling on. The chrome is recorded rather than applied because a task that says "re-import and regenerate" is not licence to restyle every surface in the SPA |
| D201 | **Seven things T082 decides about the narrow layout.** (1) **The header's chrome takes two lines of its own at 390 px, and the filter strip is the third.** 21 §Regions, narrow says the header "wraps to two rows"; measured in the shipped chrome at 390 px, the brand and version (139 px), the counts (140 px), `＋ new run` (89 px), `workflows` (85 px) and the text-size button (24 px) come to about 500 px of content, so one line for all of them exists only if something is dropped or shrunk — and the same sentence forbids both ("Nothing is dropped", and D195 forbids scaling chrome with type). What is implemented is therefore the structure the sentence is about: the chrome above, the chips and the `/` filter below it as one strip with its own horizontal scroll, wrapping as the width demands. 10 §Layout says this in the same words. (2) **`useIsNarrow()` is used in exactly three components; everything else is a `max-md:` class.** The shell (`App.tsx`) mounts the list *or* the detail, `PaneBar` renders the back control *instead of* the collapse toggle, and `RunList` draws a two-line row *instead of* a grid row: three places where the markup differs rather than the styling. The query lives once in `lib/useIsNarrow.ts` and is `(min-width: 768px)` negated, so it is Tailwind's `md` and cannot drift from it. (3) **Every overlay header carries a real close button below the breakpoint** (`aria-label="close"`, Radix's `Dialog.Close`), in place of the `esc …` hint, which is hidden there. 21 asks for one on any overlay with a header; the sheets make it load-bearing, because a full-screen panel leaves no backdrop to tap. Above the breakpoint the button is `display: none` and the hint is exactly as it was. (4) **The pane dots keep their 14×3 px bar and gain a 24×24 px transparent box around it**, rather than growing: they are a mark, and WCAG 2.5.8 is about the hit area. (5) **The library's grid columns move from an inline `style` to a class**, because a breakpoint cannot override an inline style; the generated declaration is identical at `md` and above. (6) **jsdom has no `matchMedia`, so `src/test-setup.ts` ships one** that answers `min-width` / `max-width` off `window.innerWidth` (1024 by default, so every existing test stays a desktop test) and re-answers on `resize`; `lib/__tests__/fixtures.ts` is how a test says "this one is a phone". (7) **The touch gate covers `options`-mode answers only.** 21 §Touch operation names the answer flow as "options, text and form kinds", but no workflow in `web/e2e/workflows.py` opens a `text` or a `form` request, so no spec in `web/e2e/` taps `request-text`, `request-send` or a form control at any viewport; `web/e2e/mobile.spec.ts` answers both of `probe`'s requests by their option buttons. Adding such a fixture is outside this task's file list (`docs/plans/T082-mobile-support.md` step 14 fences flow 4 to the permission option button) and the gap predates the narrow layout. In the same spirit, the `action` overlay is the one in the overlay sweep that is *opened* by link rather than by tap: no fixture workflow declares a plugin action (09 §Declarations), so the palette lists no row to tap; its containment in the viewport and its dismissal by touch are asserted as for every other overlay. | **new (2026-09-10)** | (1) is the one place 21 asks for something the mock's own type scale cannot pay for, and it was measured rather than argued: the alternative readings are all worse (moving the counts into the scrolling strip hides a number that is always on screen today; shortening `＋ new run` to an icon drops the label 21 says stays). The sentence's *purpose* — nothing hidden, one scrolling strip, no page scroll — is met exactly. (2) keeps one breakpoint in one place: a component that reads the width when a class would do is a component that renders twice and a second definition of `md` to keep in step. (3) is the difference between "closable by touch" and "closable": the two sheets have no backdrop, and the hint they carried names a key that a phone has not got. (4) is the mock's own answer for its narrow mode, and the honest one — a 24 px bar would be a different design, not a bigger target. (5) is mechanical. (6) is a test-environment gap, and the shim answers off the one thing jsdom does model, so a test states a width rather than stubbing a matcher. (7) is written down so that "the five flows pass by touch" is not read as "all three answer kinds pass by touch": the narrowing is in the fixtures, not in the layout — the request panel's text and form controls are fluid and overflow nothing at 390 px — but a gate is worth only what it runs, and the next person to widen `web/e2e/workflows.py` should find this waiting for them |
| D202 | **The `planner` workflow is removed; its two seats become nodes of `feature`.** A separate workflow that wrote plans and then queued `feature` runs meant two workflows mutating one working tree, and they wrote over each other. The graph is now `prompt ▶ prepare ▶ planner ▶ implement ▶ gate ▶ review ▶ qa ▶ approve ▶ merge`: `prompt` is haiku rewriting the request into an outcome, `planner` is opus reading the codebase and committing one `docs/plans/<title>-<slug>.md` on the branch, and `implement` resolves it through the glob it always used. The plan is verified off the branch with git rather than taken from the agent's answer, and because it is a commit, `gate` counts the implementer's commits from `plan_base` rather than from `base` — from `base` the "you made no commit" check would always pass. `workflows/checkout.py` folds back into `feature/sandbox.py`, having existed only to be shared | **new (2026-09-10)** | One tree admits one writer, and the pool that enforces that is per-workflow work, not per-tree. Writing the plan inside the run also puts it in the same merge commit as the code it describes, so a plan and its implementation can never drift, and a plan is no longer something a human has to write before submitting |
| D203 | **`approve` is unattended by default (`FEATURE_ATTENDED=0`); the node stays in the graph.** It still routes, it simply does not stop. `FEATURE_ATTENDED=1` puts the question back | **new (2026-09-10)** | Not because the question was worthless, but because waiting for it was: a `human_input` gives its worker slot back for the duration of the wait (04 §Waiting), and on a capacity-1 pool that is the one moment a stale `ready` task can claim the slot. Observed on T082 — `qa` routed to `approve`, `approve` released the slot, a `prepare` left `ready` by an earlier rerun took it and started a second `implement` lineage of the same run, and the operator's answered `merge` then queued behind an agent turn it could not outrun. The run was cancelled and the merge finished by hand |
| D204 | **Six things `⏎ focus run` decides.** (1) **`⏎` picks a run up; it no longer sends the keyboard to the detail pane**, and the `Detail` ref and `tabIndex={-1}` that served that binding go with it — `tab` is how attention reaches the detail pane, which is what D176 (1) argued for in the first place. No keycap is invented: `⏎`, `↑`, `↓`, `j`, `k` and `esc` were all in 10 §Keyboard already, and what changes is what two of them mean while a run is held. (2) **Focus is a fact about the *selected* run.** `useUi.focusedRun` holds an id, not a boolean, and the shell reads it as held only while it is `?run=`, its row is in the filtered list, the run list is not collapsed to its rail, and the viewport is at `md` or above; a derived guard decides the current render and one effect puts a stale id down, so a chip, a `/` filter, a delete, a selection change, a `b` that collapses the list or a narrow window can never leave an invisible run under the arrow keys. The collapsed rail needs the guard in `toggleRunFocus` as well as in the derivation: `Splitter` does not mount `RunList` at all when `listCollapsed` is set, and `inList()` still answers `true` for the body, so `⏎` would otherwise pick up a run that is not drawn. Selection follows the run for free, because selection *is* the run: `?run=` names an id, the rows are keyed by `row.id`, and nothing writes `?run=` on a move. (3) **`j`/`k` move too, and the reorder is not a palette row.** `lib/keys.ts` draws `↑`/`↓`/`j`/`k` as one binding — one thing the operator can do — so the mode changes all four; the table gains a `move run` row under the note "while a run is focused" rather than a new cap. The palette's two `reorder` rows stay keyless (D175 (5)): a mode is not a command, and no palette row can be one. (4) **The swap is the endpoint's `{direction}` against the true dispatch neighbour, filter or no filter.** Under an active chip or `/` filter a press can therefore move the run past a run the operator cannot see, and the visible order may not change; the toast reports the position the server settled on, which is the honest report (02 §Real data only). `{index}` computed from the visible neighbour was considered and rejected: it would make one key mean two different moves depending on a filter. The toast carries a stable per-run id (`run-position-<id>`), so holding an arrow replaces one toast rather than stacking one per press. There is no optimistic reorder and no debounce — each press posts a swap the server resolves against the row's current position. (5) **The focused row is drawn in the second accent, in the chrome and not in the fill, and the mode is announced.** Selection keeps the mock's blurple (`color-mix(accent 12%, surface)` + 2 px `--color-accent`); focus keeps that same fill and changes the chrome around it — 2 px `--color-accent-2-400` on the left border, the same width so no column shifts, plus a 1 px inset ring of the same colour — and carries `data-run-focused` — a `data-*` attribute and not ARIA, because `option` takes `aria-selected` and nothing else that means this, and `aria-grabbed` is deprecated. A fill of its own was tried first (`color-mix(accent-2 22%, surface)`) and cannot be had: `StatusPill` is transparent and paints `text-status-*` straight onto the row, that mix is `#404057` against the selection's `#303046`, and it takes `fail` (`#d9868f`) from 4.74:1 to 3.71:1 and `muted` (`#9397ab`) from 4.43:1 to 3.47:1 on a pill that is 10.5 px at normal weight and so is never WCAG large text — a `serious` axe violation, which 10 §Accessibility and quality forbids outright, and reachable because (4) allows `⏎` on a run in any status. The tint had to move rather than the text: `web/src/styles/theme.css` is generated from `docs/v1/design/nocturne.css` and `--ath-status-fail` is a bare hex with no brighter sibling. Keeping the selection's fill keeps all seven tones of 10 §Status colours at the ratio they have today. It is enforced rather than argued: `web/e2e/a11y.spec.ts` gains a fifth state — a `failed` run held by `⏎`, with the pointer moved off the row first, because `hover:bg-*` outranks the tint and a row still under the cursor is measured on its hover colour — and `web/e2e/workflows.py` gains `flop`, one node that raises with `retries=0`, to produce the run. The `muted`-on-selected 4.43:1 is older than this decision and is left alone: `--ath-status-muted` is `--color-neutral-500` and the step above it, `--color-neutral-400`, is already `queued`'s, so the only fixes are to a generated token or to the mock's own selected tint. Colour is not the only signal: the list's footer strip swaps `↑↓ select · ⏎ focus run` for `↑↓ move run · ⏎/esc done` inside a `role="status"` wrapper, with `n shown` left outside it. The mock has no focused row, so this is the boring choice from the tokens it does have. (6) **`esc` puts a run down only when no overlay is up**, the overlay being the nearer thing — and while one is up the arrows are suppressed anyway (`keys/scope.ts`). | **new (2026-09-10)** | The operator asked for v0's focus behaviour back and Tab could not give it: Tab is the browser's (D176 (1)), and taking it would strand a keyboard-only operator. `⏎` was free the moment the detail pane had `tab` to reach it, and it is the key that already meant "this row" in the list. Making focus a mode rather than a drag is what lets the reorder reuse the endpoint and the palette rows that already existed, without a fourth thing for `↑`/`↓` to mean; making it derived rather than trusted is what stops the one failure mode a mode has — arrows still armed over a row that is no longer there |
| D205 | **The gate is hermetic: the shell configures nothing, and no test talks to a server it did not start.** `compose.yaml` passes `ATHANORE_HOST` and `ATHANORE_PORT` through from the shell that starts it, and pydantic-settings reads `ATHANORE_*` above `athanore.toml` (02 §Configuration), so an operator serving on `0.0.0.0` turned `tests/api`, `tests/testing` and most of `tests/engine` red — `create_app()` refuses a non-loopback bind with no operator token (12 §Operator token) — without a line of code having changed. Two things now hold. (1) **A rootdir `conftest.py` drops every configuration name before each test**: one `ATHANORE_<FIELD>` per field of `AthanoreSettings`, the deprecated `ARTIFICER_*` fallbacks it still reads, and the CLI's `ATHANORE_URL` / `ATHANORE_TOKEN`. The set is *derived from the model*, not listed and not a prefix sweep, so a field added later is covered with no edit and a name that is not configuration cannot be swept up: `ATHANORE_IN_CONTAINER`, `ATHANORE_TEST_PG_URL`, `ATHANORE_TEST_PG_REQUIRED` and `ATHANORE_SMOKE` drive the harness itself and survive. It sits at the rootdir because `testpaths` is `tests` **and** `examples/tests`, and it publishes the set through a `config_env` fixture rather than an import, because every `conftest.py` in this tree is imported under the module name `conftest` and `from conftest import …` resolves to whichever one pytest loaded first. `tests/test_env_hygiene.py` asserts all of it. (2) **A test that reaches a default URL names a stub.** `tests/cli/test_client.py`'s unknown-verb test left `athanore no-such-verb` on the default loopback server, and T054's alias check is a real `GET /api/workflows`: on a machine running its own Athanore on 4002 that answered 401, which is exit 1 where the test wants 2. It now points `--url` at the stub the file already starts, which is the branch the test is about. | **new (2026-09-10)** | `./scripts/test.sh` is the definition of green (D74), and a gate whose verdict depends on what the developer exported or has running is not one — it is red for a reason no diff can explain, on a branch that changed no Python. The property was already claimed in the docstrings of the `settings` fixtures in `tests/api/conftest.py` and `tests/engine/conftest.py` and scrubbed for themselves by three suites; making it true once, at the rootdir, is cheaper than making every fixture pass every field |
| D206 | **Eleven things the graph pane's React Flow canvas decides.** (1) **The pane is a React Flow canvas**, `@xyflow/react` ^12 — not the deprecated `react-flow-renderer`, which was renamed to `reactflow` and then to `@xyflow/react` and is stuck at v10 — replacing the rail list of D32, which is marked superseded rather than left standing while the tree contradicts it. `GET /api/runs/{id}/graph` is unchanged: no wire change, no OpenAPI regeneration, no `web/src/api/gen` churn. (2) **One card per workflow node, and a fan-out draws branch chips inside it rather than a second card.** The forcing argument is the wire: `edges` name *nodes*, not attempts (08 §Graph semantics), so a canvas that drew `render` once per branch would have to invent which copy each arrow into and out of `render` connects — the invention 01 §Real data only forbids, and the reason the rail could get away with sub-lists (a list has no arrows). D167 (1) and (2) survive as the chips' key and their colour: a chip is keyed by its attempts' branch-frame stack and carries that branch's own state, so a fan-out mid-flight shows one chip done beside one running on a node that is `in_progress`. (3) **The layout is a pure function of `generation`, and there is no Dagre and no ELK.** `y` is the node's rank among the distinct generations present, `x` spreads a rank left to right in the order the route sent it, centred. Both halves are already on the wire (08: nodes come out in generation order and, within a generation, in declaration order; D131 (6)), so a layout engine would spend a dependency and an async measurement pass to *re-derive* the first and *reorder* the second — and reordering a rank to reduce crossings would hide an order the workflow's author wrote deliberately. Crossings are therefore not minimised, and `graphLayout` is tested without a DOM. (4) **Every node carries an explicit `width` and `height`**, which React Flow reads as already measured, so the canvas needs no measurement round trip, draws the same picture on its first frame as on its second, and renders under jsdom, where nothing has a size. Two small shims are still needed there (`DOMMatrixReadOnly`, `SVGElement.prototype.getBBox`); the inert `ResizeObserver` is unchanged. (5) **A back edge leaves and arrives on the right-hand side and is the only kind that carries a label** (`loop`), keeping the mock's right-hand rail idiom without asking the operator to read a colour; forward and `join` edges run down the ranks and a `join` edge is drawn *as* a forward edge, because the `⋈` glyph on the target (D167 (4)) and the EDGES aside already name it twice. An edge with `traversed == 0` is dashed and a step back in the neutral ramp; one with `traversed > 1` is labelled `×n`. (6) **The detail column moves inside the card, on a second line, not into a tooltip.** `attempt 2 · 105s` ticks once a second and `2 of 3 arrived` is the one thing an operator watching a fan-in is waiting for; a hover hides both, and hides them completely below the breakpoint, where there is no hover (21 §Narrow layout). D167 (3)'s precedence is unchanged. (7) **Fit on load, zoom by button, pan by drag, wheel to the pane, and no dragging nodes.** The canvas is keyed on the run id, so switching runs refits and a graph being watched is never yanked out from under a manual pan; `zoomOnScroll` is off and `preventScrolling` is off, so a wheel over the canvas scrolls the pane as it does everywhere else. Nodes are not draggable because their positions are derived from the response and a drag would snap back on the next `run.*` invalidation. Below the `md` breakpoint — a phone — the canvas is a fitted picture with no pan, no zoom and no controls, and the EDGES block underneath carries the detail, so the fit is given a zoom floor of its own there (`fitViewOptions.minZoom`), well under the interaction floor the `<Controls>` zoom out to. `getViewportForBounds` clamps the zoom it solves to `fitViewOptions.minZoom ?? minZoom`, and a rank is 112 px, so eight ranks — `examples/feature_build` — are 840 px and do not fit a 320 px canvas at 0.4: the picture would be clipped off both ends with no gesture that reaches what was cut. A picture nothing can move must be the whole picture, however small it gets, which is what the rail it replaced gave by scrolling. (8) **The React Flow attribution stays and is restyled for contrast rather than hidden.** `proOptions.hideAttribution` is the subscriber's switch and this project is not a subscriber. Its stylesheet hard-codes `#999` on a translucent grey, which composites to roughly 3.2:1 on Nocturne and would be a serious contrast violation on a page the gate runs axe over (D178), so `web/src/styles/reactflow.css` — hand-written, unlike the generated `theme.css` beside it — maps the library's `--xy-*` variables onto Nocturne tokens and puts the link on `--color-neutral-400`. The same file rewrites the two absolute font sizes the library ships — the attribution's and the edge labels', both 10 px — as `calc(10rem / 12)`, because `ramp.test.ts` sweeps `src/` and cannot see a dependency's stylesheet, and a size that stays put at `xlarge` is the mixed-scale text D195 forbids. (9) **The card keeps the rail's test ids**: `graph-row`, `graph-detail`, `data-node` and `data-state` mean exactly what they meant, so `web/e2e/support/fixtures.ts`, `run.spec.ts`, `a11y.spec.ts` and `App.test.tsx` carry over unchanged; only `fanout.spec.ts` moves, from sub-list labels to branch chips. `docs/v1/17-serial-task-plan.md` § T063e and `docs/plans/T063e-graph-rail-renderer.md` are left alone as the record of what that task shipped; 10 §Graph pane is the document that specifies the behaviour, and it is rewritten. (10) **Which of the two layouts the pane draws is decided on the measured width of the pane, not on the `md` breakpoint.** The two are not the same number: the run list, the splitter and the pane cycle all take width off the pane, so at `md` itself the pane is around 330 px and at a 900 px window around 460 px — under half of what the canvas and the EDGES aside need side by side. Read as a media query, that band put a 208 px card in a 118 px column, and at the breakpoint exactly in a 0 px one: the cards were in the DOM, clipped, with `overflow: hidden` over them, so the pane showed an operator nothing and offered no scroll and no gesture that reached them. The threshold is 556 px of pane — the canvas's 320 px flex basis, the 26 px gutter and the aside's 210 px — and under it the pane draws the phone's column order, canvas over EDGES, which is the arrangement the rail reached the same way (it wrapped, and dropped the aside underneath); what stacking does *not* take away is (11). Measured with one `ResizeObserver` (`web/src/lib/useElementWidth.ts`), which also catches the width changes a media query cannot see at all: `b` hiding the run list, and the splitter being dragged. Until it has answered — the first paint, and a pane inside a `display: none` subtree — the viewport is the fallback, because it is right at both ends of the range. The canvas is keyed on the layout as well as the run, since `fitView` is solved once and a container that changes size afterwards does not re-solve it. (11) **Whether the canvas is a fitted picture or an instrument is a question about the device, not about the number of columns.** (7) drops pan, pinch and the control strip and lowers the fit's floor to 0.05 for one stated reason — there is no gesture to recover a clipped picture with — which is a fact about a phone. Once (10) made the stacking threshold a width of the *pane*, that rule started applying to desktop windows from 768 px to about 1010 px, where an eight-rank graph fitted into the 320 px picture is drawn at `scale(0.333)`: a 69 × 19 px card, a 5 px detail line, a name at about 4 px, and no controls, no drag and no wheel to recover it — the same band the pane had just been showing nothing in, failing the same need. So the fitted picture is `stacked && narrow`, and everywhere else — including a stacked pane on a desktop — the canvas keeps `<Controls>`, `panOnDrag` and `zoomOnPinch`, and its fit keeps the interaction floor of 0.4. A clipped picture is acceptable exactly where something can move it; an illegible one is not acceptable anywhere. `web/e2e/graph.spec.ts` sweeps 768 / 900 / 1024 / 1440 driving `ladder` and asserts the control strip is there *or* the viewport's scale is at least 0.4, because "the cards are inside the canvas box" — all the sweep asserted before — is satisfied by a thumbnail exactly as well as by a picture | **new (2026-09-10)** | The operator asked for the canvas, and 10 §Stack said in as many words that React Flow was *not* used — so the choice was a documented one to reverse rather than a gap to fill, and D179's rule is that the document moves with the tree. Everything after (1) is what the brief left open, and each answer is forced by something already decided: the wire's edge vocabulary settles (2), the route's ordering guarantee settles (3), React Flow's own definition of "measured" settles (4), the `⋈` glyph and the EDGES aside settle (5), the fact that the column ticks settles (6), 21 §Narrow layout settles (7), the axe floor settles (8) and four existing files settle (9). (10) is the one thing the brief did not leave open and the first attempt got wrong anyway: a pane is not a viewport, and the only honest way to lay one out against its own contents is to measure it. (11) is the correction that fixing (10) made necessary — a rule keeps its reason when it moves, and (7)'s reason is a phone, so widening its reach to every narrow pane was widening it past what justified it. What is *not* claimed is a prettier picture: a rank's order is the author's, crossings and all |
| D207 | **"delete with the `d` key is not working" was the advertisement, not the binding, and the fix is that a cap is *drawn* as the operator has to type it.** Six things. (1) **Nothing in the delete path is broken.** Driven in the gate's own chromium: with a run selected, `Shift+D` navigates to `?overlay=delete`, the confirm appears, its `delete run` button issues `DELETE /api/runs/{id}`, the row goes and `?run=` is cleared — from the page itself, from a focused run row, from inside a request panel, from the React Flow canvas with focus in the pane (D206), while a run is held by `⏎` (D204), and immediately after each of the palette, `?`, new-run, edit-run, pick-retry and library overlays had been opened and closed. No leaked keyboard claim, and React Flow's `selectionKeyCode: 'Shift'` watcher does not `preventDefault` the `D` keydown — its `isMatchingKey` compares set sizes, so `{Shift, D}` does not match `['Shift']`. The jsdom suites asserted the same thing and passed. What the operator pressed was `d`, which `handleKey` checks before the app's own keys, finds no registered request panel for, and returns `false` without preventing: silently nothing. (2) **The label is derived from the cap, not listed.** `capLabel` in `web/src/lib/keys.ts` — a cap that is one uppercase letter is drawn with a shift chip, because a single capital cannot be typed any other way — applied at the three places the map is drawn (`components/Footer.tsx`, `overlays/Keys.tsx`, `overlays/Palette.tsx`). Today it changes `D` and nothing else; a second table listing `⇧D` beside `D` is a second thing to keep in step, and `KEY_BINDINGS`, `capOf`, `handleKey`, `PALETTE_COMMANDS` and `DeleteRun` are behaviourally untouched — the tests that assert what is *bound* pass unedited. The palette keeps `action.key` in its cmdk keywords and adds `capLabel` of it, so typing `D` and typing `⇧` both find the row. (3) **`⇧` (U+21E7) rather than `shift+D`**: the palette's key column is a 40 px grid track sized for `^r`, and the strip already draws `⏎` (U+23CE) and `↑` from the same font stack. (4) **`d` stays inert outside a request panel, with no hint, toast or fallthrough.** D51 is the reason `D` exists; spending that guard to save a keystroke would undo it, and the app has no transient-notice surface to put a hint on. (5) **The mock's `d` chip for this row (`docs/v1/design/Athanore.dc.html`, `COMMANDS` and `KEYS`) is superseded here, as its binding already was by D51** — the mock has no chip for what the app now binds. (6) **The keystroke had no browser coverage at all**, which is why the question could not be answered from the tree: `web/e2e/keyboard.spec.ts` drove `n`, `?`, `b`, `^p` and `a`, and `D` appeared in the suite only as a key that is *suppressed*. It now carries a test that presses `d`, presses `Shift+D` and deletes a `hold` run through the confirm | **new (2026-09-10)** | The operator's report was accurate and the cause was one the code could not show: every automated check of this key passed, because every one of them typed the cap the table holds rather than reading the chip the app draws. In an all-lowercase interface the case of a chip is not a signal an operator can see, and `d` is a live row of the same map one keystroke away — so the strip was advertising a keystroke that does nothing. Deriving the label keeps the one-table rule `lib/keys.ts` exists for; the browser test is what makes the answer reproducible next time |
| D208 | **TITLE and DESCRIPTION leave the overview's `kv` grid for a full-width block of their own.** Five things. (1) **The block is the pane's fourth section of five** — STATS, the meta grid, TITLE and DESCRIPTION, NODES, OUTPUTS — under the grid and above NODES, which is where the operator's sketch (`.athanore/Screenshot 2026-09-09 at 6.21.43 PM.png`) puts them and where 10 §Panes already listed DESCRIPTION: last of the meta fields, unrolled to full width rather than wedged between the tiles and the grid. It is a `dl`, one field per row, the `dt` *above* its `dd` rather than in an 86 px column beside it, because the pairing is what it already was — a term and its description, the idiom `KvPane` and the OUTPUTS list both use — and only the axis changes. (2) **The section carries no `label`, and the two `dt`s are the labels.** `Section`'s `label` is a kicker *and* the `aria-label`, and 10 §Panes has no name for a block holding these two fields; inventing one (`ABOUT`, `RUN INFO`) would put a word on screen no document asked for. The block is `data-testid="overview-about"`, after `overview-meta` and `overview-outputs`, which is a test id and not copy. It borrows `KvPane`'s own classes exactly — `text-hint` key, `text-body` value, `[overflow-wrap:anywhere]`, neutral-300 — so no size, weight or colour is invented and D195 is not touched. (3) **`formatAbout` owns the two fields and DESCRIPTION's `—` now**, and `formatMeta` skips them; the grid keeps RUN, WORKFLOW, STATUS, AGE, SESSION, AGENTS in the source's key order. `formatAbout` returns `null` when the source sent *neither* key, so an `overview`-kind panel a plugin declares — which need not be about a run at all (09 §Panel kinds) — does not grow two invented fields (01 §Real data only); an absent TITLE drops its row rather than dashing it, because 10 gives the dash to DESCRIPTION alone and the builtin's route always sends TITLE. Both values go through `formatValue` and nothing else: no preview, no clamp, no newline handling. (4) **The mock's eight-item `meta` array is superseded here**, as D207 (5) superseded its `d` chip, rather than `docs/v1/design/Athanore.dc.html` being rewritten under the tree. (5) **No wire change.** `athanore/plugins/builtin/overview.py` still sends the same eight keys and its docstring's claim — the renderer supplies the dash for the key it omits — is still exactly what happens; `tests/snapshots/openapi.json` and `web/src/api/gen` are untouched, and 09 §Panel kinds' `overview` = `dashboard` + `kv` meta + `table` is still true, because only where the pane draws two of those keys changed | **new (2026-09-10)** | A run description is written in a textarea (10 §Overlays) and can be a paragraph, and the grid it was drawn in is `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(240px, 1fr))` — so the one field most likely to be long was the one field guaranteed to be narrow. It wrapped into a column a quarter of the pane wide, pushed NODES and everything under it down the pane, and left most of the pane's width empty beside it |
| D208 | **`request.*` invalidates the run list and the run, not only that run's requests and the inbox.** 10 §Realtime's table and its transcription in `web/src/realtime/invalidate.ts` both gain `runs` and `run` on that row | **new (2026-09-10)** | `GET /api/runs` carries `pending_requests` — a correlated subquery over requests with no answer whose task is `in_progress` or `waiting` — and that is what draws the `⚠` of §Attention on a row. A permission request opens *mid-turn*, while its task is still `in_progress`, so no `task.*` event follows it; a list refetched only on `run.*`/`task.*` therefore showed nothing waiting on the operator until something unrelated moved. Found by smoke-testing the permission channel: the request was open and `pending_requests` was right, but the browser never asked again. Same reasoning as D155, one row further down |
| D209 | **A selected run can be cleared: `esc` gains a third rung, and the pane bar's back control is drawn at every width.** `esc` unwinds overlay, then held run, then selection; above the breakpoint the back control sits beside the list-collapse toggle rather than replacing it, labelled "clear the selected run". 10 §Keyboard and 21 §Narrow layout both say so | **new (2026-09-10)** | The `global` panes are shown when no run is selected, and nothing above the breakpoint could get back to that state: the only writers of `?run=` were the delete confirm — which requires destroying the run — and 21's back control, which is `max-md` only. Selecting a run was therefore a dead end, and the crontab and drop-box panes were unreachable after the first click. Both routes are added because they answer different hands: one keyboard, one pointer |

## Open questions (not blocking)

1. Should `allow_always` answers be surfaced as a per-run allowlist the
   operator can inspect and revoke? Today the agent's own rule is the
   mechanism (carried). Leaning: expose read-only in the task drawer.
2. Default retention windows (14 d transcripts, 30 d events) — revisit
   after real usage.
3. Whether the inbox should support bulk "allow all from this task".
   Leaning yes, as a plugin action on the `requests` builtin.
4. **Closed by D59 (T001).** `docs/design/*` and `docs/bugs/*` are
   git-ignored in the MVP repository. The findings v1 cites are folded
   into 20, so `docs/v1/` is self-contained and nothing depends on that
   history. Un-ignoring it for its own sake is not a v1 question.
5. Run retention. Runs never expire and `GET /api/runs` returns them
   whole; fine for months of local use, but an `archived` flag or a
   default `?limit=` will be wanted before the list stops being "small".
